  3. **W3-CAL dispatches now, items only, unchanged in scope** (R-62 a3): 30 items, all 7 rubric items at ≥ 4 each, the 0/1/2 range, provenance in the manifest (design `:194-195`). The manifest row gains one provenance field, `intended_score` (the score the author wrote the item to carry). Its only reader is the R-58 c5 range check (`rulings.md:731`), which without labels has no other evidence. It is **provenance, not a label**: no κ, agreement or header row is ever computed from it (condition 3).
  4. **`tools/calibrate.py` runs without a labels file.** §11's label-before-verdict invariant (design `:529-536`, `HB-CAL-001`) is amended: it applies **when `bench/calibration/C1/labels.yaml` exists**, all-or-none. When the file is absent, the tool runs the inter-judge calibration, `calibration.started` records `labels_sha256: null` (design `:200`, `:537`), and the human half is the NOT_RECORDED reason above. A labels file with any defect (a duplicate, 29 rows, an unknown id, a score of 3) is still refused before any spawn; a partial file is never "some labels".
  5. **A later human labelling costs no call and no version.** κ and agreement are derived from the calibration ledger's recorded rows plus the labels file whose sha256 is named (design `:210-212`); the verdicts are stored per judge (design `:437-445`), so labels that arrive after the calibration pass complete the human half with 0 backend calls. Labels are not in `catalog_hash` (the recipe is `metrics.yaml`, `bench/rubrics/**` and the judge entries: `rulings.md:742`, design `:238`) and κ is not in `views.export` (`views.py:580-592`: cells, scores, leaderboard only; spec `:545` "No threshold"), so a labels file lands under `0.4` without moving a score, a composite or the gate's byte-equality. Because `runs/` is not committed (`.gitignore:15`), the verdict rationales stay on this machine; a labeller who reads them is not blind, so R-58 c5's "before" becomes a disclosure (condition 4).
  6. **US-35 c3** stays open, as R-58 and design `:548` already carry it: a named wave-4 row whose trigger is now "a human labeller exists", with the operator's 2026-09-25 decision recorded as the reason it did not close in wave 3. Its inter-judge half closes in `0.4` iff R-70 3(a)–(c) land before the freeze; otherwise it closes with the Copilot entry at `0.5`. **The report header** (design §12, the Calibration row at `:559`): `n = 30 · inter-judge κ: <value with exact agreement> | not recorded: second judge not qualified · vs human labels: not recorded: no human labels (operator declined 2026-09-25)`, plus the R-62 a3 disclosure sentence (design `:550-551`). **The `0.4` freeze does not wait** for labels (the same shape as R-70 5 for Copilot): R-59 c3's four preconditions (`rulings.md:744`) are untouched by this ruling; a labels file after the freeze is a header change, not a catalog change.
- **Reasoning:**
  - The spec promise is a human ground truth; the operator is the only human in the run and declined. The honest state is the one R-58 already named as the fallback ("inter-judge κ only, human half `not recorded`", `rulings.md:724`), reached one decision earlier than planned. A blank prompts the question a reader should ask; a third model's number would end it wrongly (IO8).
  - What calibration still buys without labels: the 30 items are the inter-judge κ set (ADR-0009:47, "the calibration set as the verifier"; R-58 c5's coverage of every rubric item and score), the stale-calibration binding (design `:539-541`; `request.py:70`), and one judge's stored verdicts that a second judge completes later with no repeat call (R-63 (b)). None of that needs a label, so W3-CAL is on the critical path today (R-62 reasoning, `rulings.md:775`) and waits for nothing.
  - A third-vendor reference is not forbidden as a *new* measure: if a comparison ever wants it, it is its own header row named for its source (`agreement with <vendor> reference`, disclosed, no threshold), through a qualified tool-less invocation, at `0.5` or later, and it never inherits the name "human labels". Nobody has asked for it; the ladder stops at YAGNI.
  - `intended_score` is the smallest evidence that makes R-58 c5's range condition checkable at all; its risk is that a later slice reads it as a label, so the control is a test, not this prose (CI6; condition 3).
- **Conditions:**
  1. **Strings and states.** The human-half reason is `no human labels (operator declined 2026-09-25)`; `labels pending` is retired from design §11 `:547` before s5 builds it (the design is W3-GW-D's; the one-line edit is assigned to the s5 slice). `calibration.started` carries `labels_sha256: null` when no file exists (IO: explicit, never omitted).
  2. **HB-CAL-001 is file-conditional.** T-GW-16 (design `:536`) keeps its four refusals on a present file and gains two rows: an absent file spawns, with the human half NA `no human labels`; an empty file (0 rows) is refused, not treated as absent.
  3. **`intended_score` never becomes a label.** `bench validate` (or the manifest check in `tools/calibrate.py`) reads it only to assert every rubric item has ≥ 4 items and every score in {0, 1, 2} appears; a test asserts that with the manifest present and `labels.yaml` absent, the header's human half is NOT_RECORDED and no κ is computed from the manifest. The W3-CAL brief states the field's purpose in one line.
  4. **Blinding is disclosed, not assumed.** `labels.yaml` gains `labelled_utc` per row (already in the grain, design `:197`) and one file-level `blind: true|false` attestation by the labeller. When the labels' earliest `labelled_utc` is later than `calibration.started`, the header shows `labels post-date the calibration verdicts` beside the human half; `blind: false` or absent shows `labeller blinding: not recorded`. R-58 c5's "labels are made before the operator sees any judge verdict" is amended to this disclosure.
  5. **Plan and run record.** The Leader amends the W3-CAL row (`coordination-finish-harness-bench.md:237`) from "The labels are **HUMAN** (the operator, R-58)" to "no labels in `0.4` (R-72); a human labeller when one exists; never a model", and the wave-4 row for US-35 c3 names both halves and their triggers. The run record's carried item (`coordination-finish-harness-bench-run.md:152`) closes by citing this ruling; the W3-CAL dispatch happens after that edit and before the freeze.
  6. **Header proof.** T-GW-29's header case (design `:557-559`) gains the no-labels state; the R-62 a3 disclosure sentence is present in every judge-pass header whether or not labels exist.
  7. **Class, not instance (CI).** `docs/lessons/defect-classes.md` gains "a ground-truth quantity whose human source is unavailable, re-sourced from a model and reported under the human name" as a sibling of the plausible-wrong-number class; the control is condition 3's test and the rule that a header row's name states its source. Sweep: the scripted-user matcher's labelled question set (spec `:457`) is the one other human-labelled set in the spec; its labels' provenance is checked at GR-CLAR's join, and a model-labelled set there is disclosed under its own name or NOT_RECORDED.

## R-73 · 2026-09-25 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-F1-1: (b) per-vendor `model_map`, decided by the operator; keys `<role>@<vendor>`, resolved per cell by the cell's vendor; the Leader stipulates the OpenAI roles; US-21 moves from the cell to the metric; F1 stays in the Copilot-versus-Codex comparison

- **Ruling:** **(b), by the operator's decision of 2026-09-25** (relayed by the Leader; recorded here, not re-decided). `tasks/F1/task.yaml`'s `model_map` becomes a per-vendor map with **flat keys `<role>@<vendor>`** — `domain-model@anthropic`, `derived-quantities@anthropic`, `tests@anthropic`, `domain-model@openai`, `derived-quantities@openai`, `tests@openai` — one value per key, every role present for every vendor the profiles declare. Each cell routes every role to a model of **its own vendor**; a cell never sees the other vendor's column as an allowance. F1 stays in the smoke BOM (`bench/bom.yaml:47`) and in the headline comparison (Copilot versus Codex on `gpt-6-sol`, Claude Code on `claude-opus-5-5` third). (a) is rejected: it would drop the headline pair from the one scenario the proposal added a harness requirement for (`cross-harness-benchmarking-proposal.md:200`). (c) is rejected: a nested schema is `src/**` work that buys nothing the flat vendor-qualified key does not, and today's resolver reads values only (`views.py:433-437`), so (b) is the smallest change that also fixes the resolver.
  1. **Vendor is a profile fact, not a string rule.** `bench/profiles/<harness>.yaml` gains `vendor:` (`claude-code` → `anthropic`; `codex`, `copilot` → `openai`), loaded into `Profile` (`profiles.py:41-56`, `:135-151`). The map's vendor set must equal the profiles' vendor set. A vendor is never inferred from a model-id prefix.
  2. **Resolution, one definition.** One function (in `plan` or `normalize`, used by `views._mapped` and by `grade/coordination.py`) returns, for a cell, `{role: model}` from the keys whose suffix is the cell's profile vendor, and only those. `views.py:424`'s served-model allowance reads that set. Today's `_mapped` (`views.py:436-437`) would admit `claude-sonnet-5` as an allowed served model in a Codex cell; after this ruling that cell is `invalid (model mismatch)` (HB-VAL-002).
  3. **The Anthropic column stands as authored:** `domain-model@anthropic: claude-opus-5-5`, `derived-quantities@anthropic: claude-sonnet-5`, `tests@anthropic: claude-sonnet-5` (`task.yaml:29-31`); `claude-haiku-4-5` stays excluded, it is the auxiliary model (`claude-code.yaml:24`; `oracle/README.md:45`). The `assume:` at `task.yaml:25-27` is retired by citing this ruling.
  4. **The OpenAI column: the Leader stipulates (R-33), in the same shape.** `domain-model@openai: gpt-6-sol` — the vendor's most capable model as the register already reads it (R-58, `rulings.md:722`; spec `:666`) and the pin of `copilot-sol` and `codex-sol` — and **one workhorse id for both** `derived-quantities@openai` and `tests@openai`, `gpt-6-astra` or `gpt-6-luna`, chosen by a **check, not recall** (OpenAI's own model tiering read this session, cited in the audit entry) from the three ids the Codex recording advertises (`oracle/README.md:44`, `tests/fixtures/acp/recordings/codex-x1.jsonl`). The choice is written in `task.yaml`, in `prompt.md`'s table, and in the run record. `assume:` Copilot serves the same three OpenAI ids as Codex; **confirm:** R-74 c6's Copilot qualification turn records the served model of a sub-agent asked for the workhorse id; **breaks:** if Copilot cannot serve it, the workhorse is the other of the two, and if neither, R-74 c6's routing result for Copilot decides (item 6 below).
  5. **Shape rule, enforced at plan time.** For every scenario-6 cell, at least one resolved role names a model other than the cell's pin, else `bench plan` refuses the plan with an HB code: a map whose every role equals the pin cannot distinguish routing from no routing. (On both vendors `domain-model` equals the pin and two roles are off it; that is the treatment.)
  6. **US-21 is amended from the cell to the metric.** The spec's plan-time `not_applicable (routing unsupported)` (`harness-bench.md:404-405`) is not built (no match in `src/`) and rests on an issue-tracker inference (`:405` `[Inferred: Copilot CLI issue 2939]`; proposal `:200`). Under this ruling a harness on which a sub-agent **measurably** cannot be started on a model other than the session's (R-74 c6) keeps its F1 cells; only **`model_map_adherence`** is NOT_RECORDED for those cells with the reason `routing unsupported (<qualification cell id>)` (`metrics.yaml:6`, `:97`; US-27 `:440`), and every other coordination metric is measured. A missing harness feature is still never scored as a failure, which is what US-21 asks; the exclusion is triggered by a measurement, never by a citation.
- **Reasoning:**
  - The prompt is one text for every combo (US-10 `:352`; `tasks/README.md:29`), and a Codex or Copilot cell cannot serve an Anthropic id (`oracle/README.md:44`). With one flat map, F1 was either Claude-only (a) or scored on a routing the cell cannot perform; (b) keeps one prompt and one task version while each cell reads only its own vendor's column.
  - The value-only resolver is the defect the key shape exposes: `_mapped` folds every vendor's ids into one allowance (`views.py:437`), so the per-vendor key without item 2 widens US-11 (`:356-357`) for every scenario-6 cell. One resolver used by the validity view and the grader is DM7 (derive once) applied; two parsers of the same key is the DM-A signature (`defect-classes.md:385`).
  - The plan already freezes the map verbatim (`plan.py:147-150`; seam S3, `phase2-stop-decisions.md:82`) and the task's scenario (`plan.py:286`), so the change is the key grammar, the resolver and the profile field; no new plan field.
  - Item 5 is the discrimination check the oracle's `evidence.md` gives the hidden tests, applied to the routing measure: a measure that reads the same on the null hypothesis is not a measure.
  - Item 6: the operator's decision keeps F1 on every combo; US-21's intent (never a failure for a missing feature) is met at the metric grain, and the coordination metrics that do not need routing (per-agent attribution, intent-log completeness, handoff fidelity, MAST, overhead, parallel efficiency; `metrics.yaml:97-105`) are exactly what the headline pair should be compared on.
- **Conditions:**
  1. **`bench validate` (`config.py:234-235`, extended):** a scenario-6 map is refused when any key is bare (no `@`), names a vendor no profile declares, omits a role for any declared vendor, names a value that is an auxiliary model of that vendor's harness, or names a value no harness of that vendor serves (the served list is the harness's own recording or the R-74 qualification record, never memory). Red first on today's `task.yaml:28-31`.
  2. **Resolver test (W2-VIEWS' owner):** with the F1 map frozen in a plan, `_mapped` for a `codex-sol` cell is exactly the OpenAI values and for a `cc-opus` cell exactly the Anthropic values; a Codex record serving `claude-sonnet-5` is HB-VAL-002; a Codex record serving the OpenAI workhorse is `valid`. The coordination grader's role comparison (`oracle/README.md:57`) calls the same resolver; `test_architecture.py` (or a grep test) asserts no second parser of `@`.
  3. **The Leader's stipulation** (item 4) lands in `task.yaml`, `prompt.md` and the audit entry in one commit with the citation of the tiering source; a later change of the workhorse id is a new task version (spec `:237`), never an edit under the freeze.
  4. **`prompt.md`** (`:7`, `:9-13`, `:15`): the routing table gains two model columns (`Anthropic model`, `OpenAI model`); the sentence "on the model named below" becomes "on the model named for your vendor"; the substitution rule at `:15` is unchanged. Identical text for every combo; no per-vendor prompt file. The edit is TASKS-c's (`coordination-finish-harness-bench.md:165`) and it moves F1 to `ready` (`oracle/README.md:12`) together with R-74's profile work; F1 joins `bench/task-freeze.yaml` at its join (B1's precedent, `00d6bde`).
  5. **Spec edit:** the spec's owner amends US-21 (`:404-405`) to the metric-level form of item 6, citing this ruling; the `[Inferred: … 2939]` tag is replaced by the measured result per harness once R-74 c6 has run.
  6. **What the smoke run's F1 cells measure, and what the report discloses.** The cells run on every combo of a run that has passed R-74's gates. They are graded by the registered graders only — `correctness` (the 10 hidden xUnit tests, `oracle/README.md:24`), `cost`, `process`, `drift` (`grade/runner.py:68`, `:89-91`); `judge` metrics read `no rubric for this task` (`judge.py:40`, `:80`; the `0.4` judged scope is C1, R-62); the whole **coordination area is NOT_RECORDED `not built`** (`grade/coordination.py:15-16` raises `NotImplementedError`; not in `GRADERS`). The facts a later pass needs are recorded now — served model per native session including sub-agents (ADR-0008:49; R-74 c4), `tool_calls` by class, the resolved map in the plan — so a `0.5` coordination pass grades the smoke F1 cells with **0 model calls** (the R-63 (b) / R-72 5 shape). The report header carries, per combo, the resolved `role → model` for its vendor, and per cell the served-model set beside it; a role whose map model equals the pin is labelled `non-discriminating` beside its value. The F1 rows carry the banner `coordination: not built in 0.4` so an empty area is never read as a zero.
  7. **Class, not instance (CI):** `docs/lessons/defect-classes.md` gains "a treatment parameter authored in one vendor's ids and applied to every combo" (sibling of PERM-A, `:261`); the control is condition 1's per-vendor completeness rule. Sweep: `model_map` is the only per-role parameter in `bench-task/1` (`config.py:234`); `auxiliary_models` is already per harness (`profiles.py:53`); no other task field names a model.

## R-74 · 2026-09-25 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-F1-2: a scenario-6 tool allowance per harness, decided by the operator — class `delegate`; Claude Code `Agent`; Copilot `task`, `write_agent`, `read_agent`, `list_agents`; Codex once measured; in profile only in a scenario-6 cell, out of profile everywhere else, red-first; no F1 cell before the per-harness qualification

- **Ruling:** **By the operator's decision of 2026-09-25** (relayed by the Leader; recorded here, not re-decided): a scenario-6 cell may start sub-agents. The allowed ids are **Claude Code `Agent`**; **Copilot `task`, `write_agent`, `read_agent`, `list_agents`**; **Codex: its collaboration tools, allowed only after they are measured** (item 5). The allowance applies to **scenario-6 tasks only**, is carried by the profile per cell, and has a **red-first allowlist test**. The alternative disposition — F1 measures coordination through the intent log without sub-agents — is rejected: `model_map_adherence` and `per_agent_attribution` (`metrics.yaml:97-98`) are defined over sub-agent sessions (`oracle/README.md:57-58`), and a scenario that forbids the mechanism it scores measures nothing (the request's own consequence, `oracle/README.md:52`).
  1. **The class is `delegate`.** A fourth class beside ADR-0004's three and R-54's `meta`: a call that starts, addresses or reads a sub-agent and, by itself, reads and writes nothing in the workspace. Readers: `claude_code.py:32` `TOOL_CLASSES` gains `"Agent": "delegate"`; `copilot.py:65-70` `TOOL_CLASS` gains the four ids; `codex.py:53-58` gains the measured ids; `views.py:346-347` gains `DELEGATE`. `Agent` leaves `OUT_OF_PROFILE` (`test_allowlist_classes.py:48-52`) for a new `DELEGATE` set; `task`, `write_agent`, `read_agent`, `list_agents` leave `COPILOT_OUT_OF_PROFILE` (`:68-69`) likewise. `ListAgents`, `SendMessage`, `TaskStop`, `Workflow` (Claude Code; `tools-2.1.282-win32.jsonl:3` and its deferred delta) stay out of profile: the operator's list is the allowance, and any attempt at another id is a decision request, never a widening (R-54 (b): refused = HB-VAL-009 on a valid cell; executed = HB-VAL-008).
  2. **Validity rule, one emitter (R-47).** In `views._out_of_profile` (`views.py:359`): an executed `delegate` row is in profile **iff** the cell's frozen task scenario is 6 (`plan.py:286` `tasks[<id>].scenario`); then it is counted per cell as `delegate_calls`, a cost axis beside `meta_calls` (`views.py:130`), never scored. In any other cell an executed `delegate` row is HB-VAL-008 `invalid (out-of-profile tool called)` and a refused one HB-VAL-009 — the existing codes at their existing levels. The class is static in the reader; the scenario rule lives in the view only.
  3. **Profile per cell, not per harness file.** Claude Code: `Agent` joins `permissions.allow` (`claude-code.yaml:17`) **only** when the cell's scenario is 6 — `Profile.seed_home` (`profiles.py:61-66`) and `ProfileLauncher.seed` (`:178-179`; called at `engine.py:643`) take the cell, and the template gains a per-cell placeholder present only then (R-51 c1's shape). Copilot: `task write_agent read_agent list_agents` are appended to `--available-tools` (`copilot.yaml:7`) in `Profile.argv` (`profiles.py:83-104`) only for scenario 6, the shape of the scripted-user addition (`:99-103`). Codex: whatever item 5 measures (a `[features]` key in the seeded `config.toml`, `codex.yaml:9-13`, or nothing). Every other cell is seeded exactly as today.
  4. **Sub-agent records are read, or the allowance is withheld on that harness.** A cell "can hold several sessions (sub-agents)", keyed by native session id (ADR-0008:49); the served model and the tool calls of every sub-agent must reach `model_calls` and `tool_calls`, or the out-of-profile control is blind inside sub-agents and adherence is unmeasurable. Claude Code's `record_glob` matches the main session file only (`claude-code.yaml:22`). `assume:` build 2.1.282 writes each sub-agent's transcript as its own file under the session's folder in `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/projects/`; **confirm:** item 6's turn lists that tree after one delegated turn and the reader is pointed at it; **breaks:** no per-sub-agent record → sub-agent calls are `not recorded`, and the Claude Code allowance is **withheld** until a record source exists (rigor above speed). Copilot: sub-agent rows carry a top-level `agentId` (`copilot.py:32-35`, `:98`); `ModelCall` rows (`:179`) gain the agent id, and `promptCacheBreakState.models` (R-45 b) lists every served model. Codex: whether a spawned agent writes its own rollout (`codex.py:1`) is item 5's measurement.
  5. **Codex: measured before allowed.** The pinned 0.156.0 has a collaboration mechanism in its own record: the system message names `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, `list_agents`, called as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, with `model` settable on a non-fork spawn ("full-history forks inherit the parent model … only set `model` … when explicitly", `tests/fixtures/native/codex/ok.jsonl:4`); `turn_context` carries `multi_agent_version` (`ok.jsonl:8`) and `world_state` carries `multi_agent_mode: {mode: "explicitRequestOnly"}` (`pack-on.jsonl:7`). Today the reader classes any of those as `other` (`codex.py:53-58`) and an executed one invalidates. **One Codex qualification turn** (a Q1-style fixture, `gpt-6-sol`, R-33) asks the agent to start one sub-agent on the OpenAI workhorse id (R-73 item 4) that writes one file, and records: the `function_call` names issued (`codex.py:39`, `:118`); the spawned agent's served model and where its rollout lands; whether the spawn completed without the companion ids; and, from `codex.exe features list` (the spike-n5 pattern, `spike-n5-codex-skill-roots.md:38-46`), whether a key turns collaboration off for non-scenario-6 cells. The Codex `delegate` set is the measured ids, nothing recalled. Until that turn, a Codex cell that spawns is invalid as today, and the report says so. The turn is a Codex turn and runs in R-69 c2's Codex window (after the reset); the Codex F1 third is in run 2 only if the turn passes first.
  6. **One qualification turn per harness on the scenario-6 profile before any F1 cell** (R-45 c1's pattern; R-57's gate shape): the probe asks the coordinator to start one sub-agent on the vendor's workhorse id and have it write one file. Recorded: the `delegate` rows; **0 executed `other` rows**; the sub-agent's served model — **routing supported or not**, the datum R-73 item 6 consumes; the sub-agent's own tool calls, read (item 4); for Copilot, `tools_advertised` showing the four ids in the scenario-6 shape while the committed fixed-profile fixture (`tests/fixtures/native/copilot/fixed`, `test_allowlist_classes.py:117-122`) shows them absent; for Claude Code, whether `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` (`claude-code.yaml:19`) forces the sub-agent onto the pin. If it does, routing is `unsupported` on Claude Code under this profile (R-73 item 6 applies) and the Leader may raise a decision request on the pin mechanism (`set_model`, `profiles.py:54`) — a measured gap, not a guess.
- **Reasoning:**
  - The profile denies "any other tool the harness offers" (ADR-0004:54-59, as amended) for every scenario, and scenario 6 is the one scenario whose measures need such a tool. A per-scenario allowance is the narrowest exception: the class is named, the ids are listed per harness, the cell that may use them is identified by a frozen plan fact (`plan.py:64`, `:286`), and every other cell keeps today's profile byte for byte. An allowance keyed on the harness file would widen every cell; keyed on the prompt it would be the model's choice (US-14 `:377`).
  - A `delegate` call is not a shell, read or edit; classing it into one of those would hide it from the count and change the meaning of the per-class tallies. Its own class, counted and never scored, is R-54 (a)'s treatment of `meta` applied.
  - The scenario rule belongs in the view, not the reader: a reader row is a fact about the record (this id was called); "allowed here" is a judgement over the plan (one code, one level, one emitter; R-47). Two readers with a scenario switch would be two definitions of one quantity.
  - Sub-agent records are the load-bearing unknown. The tool profile is only a control where it is measured (R-45's lesson, PERM-B `defect-classes.md:270`): a sub-agent that can call `WebFetch` unseen is the same class as a "safe" tool with no permission callback. That is why item 4 withholds the allowance on a harness whose sub-agent records cannot be read, rather than disclosing the hole.
  - Codex is allowed only by measurement (SEED-A, `defect-classes.md:367`): the ids above are read from the build's own prompt, which is evidence that they exist, not that a spawn completes, routes, or leaves a rollout.
  - The alternative "intent log only" would also make F1 pack-on-only in effect (the intent log is source `P`, `metrics.yaml:100`; pack-off is NA by design, `oracle/README.md:59`), which would remove the pack effect from the one scenario built to show it.
- **Conditions:**
  1. **Red-first allowlist tests** (`tests/test_allowlist_classes.py`): `claude_allowlist(tmp_path)` (`:146-152`) gains a scenario parameter; for scenario ≠ 6 the allowlist ⊆ `CLASSES` (`:167-168`) holds unchanged and contains no `DELEGATE` id; for scenario 6 it is exactly `CLASSES ∪ {"Agent"}` and still disjoint from `OUT_OF_PROFILE`. `copilot_allowlist()` (`:85-90`, `:101-108`) gets the mirror: the four ids present only in the scenario-6 argv, and `assert allowed & COPILOT_OUT_OF_PROFILE == set()` still holds because the ids move to `COPILOT_DELEGATE`. `test_every_tool_id_the_pinned_build_advertises_is_classified` (`:171-174`) and the `meta` test (`:177-180`) gain `DELEGATE` in `known`, disjoint from `META` and `OUT_OF_PROFILE`. Red today: `Agent` is in `OUT_OF_PROFILE`.
  2. **View test (W2-VIEWS' owner):** the same `tool_calls` rows with one executed `Agent` row under a plan whose task scenario is 5 → `invalid (out-of-profile tool called)` HB-VAL-008; under scenario 6 → `valid` with `delegate_calls = 1`; a refused `Agent` row under scenario 5 → HB-VAL-009 on a valid cell. The one-code-one-level test of R-47 c1 stays green.
  3. **Reader tests:** each reader classes its ids as `delegate` from a synthetic row (Claude Code `Agent`; Copilot the four; Codex the measured ids), and each reader's class-coverage set gains `delegate`.
  4. **Records:** item 4's reader work lands before any F1 cell on that harness; the ledger key already admits several native sessions per cell (`views.py:50-51` `model_calls` and `tool_calls` keys carry `native_session_id`). Tokens: on Claude Code (`usage_source: acp_turn`, `claude-code.yaml:23`) whether the ACP turn total includes sub-agent calls is unmeasured; item 6's turn compares the ACP total with Σ over all session records (HB-VAL-005's cross-check, `views.py:22-23`); a mismatch makes sub-agent tokens `not recorded` with the reason, never a partial sum (R-21 c2), and the header discloses it.
  5. **Gates before the F1 cells (per harness, R-57's shape):** c1–c3 green; item 4's records read on that harness; item 6's turn passed with 0 executed `other` rows; for Codex, item 5 first. F1 is in a run's matrix only when every combo of that run has passed; otherwise F1 is left out of that run and the banner reads `F1: not run (delegation not qualified on <harness>)`; a later F1-only run on the same pins (R-69 c1's shape) joins the smoke result. The smoke run does not wait for F1.
  6. **What the smoke run's F1 cells measure, and what the report discloses.** With the allowance: validity under the four classes plus `delegate`; `delegate_calls` per cell; sub-agents started per cell with each one's served model; correctness, cost, process and drift as R-73 c6 states; the coordination area `not built` in `0.4`. A cell that starts no sub-agent is **valid** and reads `no sub-agent started` — a measured behaviour under an allowance that made delegation possible, and the datum the later adherence grade reads as 0 on every role (`oracle/README.md:57`). The header carries `scenario-6 allowance: claude-code Agent · copilot task, write_agent, read_agent, list_agents · codex <measured ids | not qualified>` and per harness `routing: supported | unsupported (<qualification cell id>) | not measured`. A report on a run with no F1 cells prints the c5 banner.
  7. **ADR-0004** gains an amendment note citing this ruling: the scenario-6 exception to "any other tool the harness offers", the ids per harness, the class, and the per-cell carriage; the Claude Code and Copilot rows list the scenario-6 additions verbatim. R-45 c5's `Skill` finding is unchanged.
  8. **Class, not instance (CI):** `docs/lessons/defect-classes.md` gains "a scenario's required capability denied by the profile every scenario shares" (sibling of PERM-A, `:261`); the control is c1's scenario-parameterised allowlist test. Sweep: scenario 1's `scripted_user` is the one other scenario-conditional profile addition and already follows the per-cell shape (`profiles.py:99-103`, R-51 c1); scenario 7's toolchain is a workspace fact, not a tool profile (`bom.yaml:49-51`).

## R-75 · 2026-09-25 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-MUT-1: (a) the flag goes; a red baseline is disclosed, not fatal; the initial failing count is recorded per cell; the Inferred kill-attribution claim becomes a seeded fixture; the all-own-tests-red case is scored as is under a `simplify:`, never silently

- **Ruling:** **(a).** `--break-on-initial-test-failure` is removed from the Stryker argv (`mutation.py:177`). Tests that are red on the **unmutated** grading copy do not make the metric NA; Stryker runs, and the score is read from `mutation-report.json` exactly as today (`mutation.py:215-235`). The definition at `phase3-graders.md:355-358` is unchanged. **(b) is rejected:** it moves the D1 task hash, invalidates the frozen D1 and the `row15-d1-1` gate run (spec `:237`; R-67 c1), and does not remove the environment-dependent red tests, so it buys a new baseline that is still red. **(c) is rejected:** it ships the metric NA on every cell of the only mutation task, against the design's own expected count `mutation_score` 5/6 for D1 (`phase3-graders.md:747`).
  1. **The red baseline is recorded, not inferred.** The grader reads Stryker's `WRN <n> tests are failing` line and writes `initial_failing_tests: <n>` to `mutation.log` and beside the score in its evidence. When the line is absent the field is `not recorded`, never `0`, until the seeded fixture (item 3) shows what 4.16.0 prints at zero failures; then and only then an absent line reads `0`.
  2. **`mutation run failed: <exit code>` stays** for what it now means: a non-zero exit with the flag gone (a build failure of the grading copy, a Stryker crash), not a red baseline. `thresholds.break` stays `0` (`mutation.py:167`) so a low score never becomes an exit code.
  3. **The Inferred claim is promoted by a fixture, not by a log read.** "Stryker excludes initially failing tests from kill attribution" was observed on one cell and is Inferred. It becomes a **seeded fixture**: D1 reference plus a **new** test project holding one test that always fails and one test that never calls `Compute` → exit `0`, `initial_failing_tests: 1`, score `0.0000` exact, and the failing test's name absent from every `killedBy` in the report. Verified means observed on the pinned 4.16.0, in the grading environment; the Leader's proposed slow-ring name check against the break-run log is not built (it needs the flag it removes).
  4. **The 5 D1 cells become characterization values,** graded twice in the slow ring and equal (`phase3-graders.md:363`, unchanged). The `35af…` → `no tests written` exact fixture is unchanged. The 74/75 flap is a property of the vendored suite; the twice-equal rule is the control that it does not reach the score (`c3d40fa1377ba0dc`: 0.8095 twice).
  5. **The design's `simplify:` at `:358` has reached its ceiling** ("a task that edits vendored tests") on the first real task. It is amended, not removed: "Ceiling reached by D1 (R-75): the score counts kills by vendored tests in the changed project. Upgrade at `0.5`: attribute kills to tests in the files the cell added or changed, from the report's `killedBy` and test locations, if a comparison reads `mutation_score` as the cell's own test quality."
  6. **A cell whose own new tests are all red is scored as is in `0.4`,** disclosed under the same `simplify:`. The NA `cell tests fail before mutation` is **not built**: detecting it needs the failing test names, which 4.16.0 prints only under the removed flag, so the detector would be a second Stryker run per cell, the whole cost of the metric again. Its evidence in `0.4` is item 1's per-cell count beside the score, and the D1 baseline count recorded once per task (item 7). Upgrade trigger: any graded cell whose `initial_failing_tests` exceeds the task baseline by more than the flake band (1), or the `0.5` attribution in item 5, whichever comes first; then the NA is defined and red-first on that cell.
- **Reasoning:**
  - The flag was a spike choice on a fixture that did not have the task's shape: the spike tested a new project `D1.HiddenTests` with no vendored test (`spike-gr-code-stryker.md:93`); D1 edits `AiDe.Core.Tests` (2681 tests) in a vendored subset with no `AiDe.sln` and no `docs/` (`tasks/D1/task.yaml` `vendored_paths`), so 74–75 tests are red before any cell acts. A precondition that fails on the task's own baseline measures the fixture, not the cell.
  - The definition (`:357`) is a ratio over mutant statuses; a green baseline is not in it. The observed run kept the ratio well-formed (Killed 17, Survived 2, NoCoverage 2; CompileError and Ignored excluded, as the definition excludes them) and 0 of 14 killers were baseline-red tests. That last fact is load-bearing and only Inferred, so it is made a fixture (CI6), not prose.
  - (a) carries no `assume:` about the toolchain; (b) rests on the belief that vendoring the solution greens the suite, which the environment tests (`AgentLaunchSurvivesIntegrationTests`) already falsify. Under (a) no task hash moves and the gate run stays valid; only the grader moves.
  - Item 6 is honest about the gap rather than modeling around it (IO): the number the reader sees is what the definition says it is, and the count beside it is the evidence that would prompt the question.
- **Conditions:**
  1. **Red-first:** `test_grade_mutation.py:221` flips to assert the flag is **absent**; a row asserts `initial_failing_tests` is parsed from a `WRN 75 tests are failing` line and is `not recorded` when the line is absent; item 3's seed lands as a fixture with its three exact assertions before the 5 D1 cells are re-graded.
  2. **Design edit** (W3-GR-CODE's owner): `phase3-graders.md:354` names the removed flag and cites this ruling and `spike-gr-code-stryker.md:93`; `:358` carries the amended `simplify:` of item 5 and the item 6 disclosure; `:360` keeps every NA reason (none added); the spike note gains one line saying its fixture did not cover a red vendored baseline.
  3. **Grader build and regrade:** whether `grader_build()` (R-59 c4) covers `mutation.py` is checked, not recalled; if it does, the regrade of `row15-d1-1` is the R-71 c1 shape (characterization values recorded, twice equal, `expected-counts.yaml` D1 `mutation_score` 5/6 confirmed). `catalog_hash` is untouched (`metrics.yaml` and `bench/rubrics/` only, `:266-269`); no version bump.
  4. **Task baseline, once per task:** the D1 baseline `initial_failing_tests` (74–75, flake band 1) is recorded in the run record and in the report header's D1 row as `baseline red tests: 74–75 (vendored subset; AiDe.sln and docs/ absent)`, so a per-cell count is read against it, never alone.
  5. **Class, not instance (CI):** `docs/lessons/defect-classes.md` gains "a grader precondition adopted from a spike whose fixture lacked the real task's shape (new test project vs. edited vendored suite)", sibling of MOD-A; the control is that every grader spike names the frozen task tree it ran on, or the design's `simplify:` names the task that reaches its ceiling. Sweep at the join: the other D1 graders that run the vendored tree (`build_and_suite_clean`, `static_analysis_delta`) are checked for the same green-baseline belief.
  6. **Report disclosure:** every D1 `mutation_score` value carries `initial_failing_tests` beside it; a cell above the baseline band is flagged `red tests added` in the row, unscored, so a reader is never handed the bare number.

## R-76 · 2026-09-26 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-GATE-7: (a) a pinned, append-only known-findings allowance for criterion 7, valid only for paths no grader reads; `bench verify` itself forgives nothing; the report discloses it; the allowance dies with the run it names

- **Ruling:** **(a).** `check_regrade.py` criterion 7 gains a committed allowance of exact `(run, code, cell, path)` tuples, the two named here and no others: `row15-d1-1 · HB-LED-005 · 35af195cfe821dca · ws/.git/index` and `row15-d1-1 · HB-LED-005 · c3d40fa1377ba0dc · ws/.git/index`. An allowed error is written to `report.notes` as `verify error allowed (R-76): …`, never dropped; any other error is still `criterion 7: …` in `report.failures`. **(b) is rejected:** a paid D1 re-capture re-freezes `expected-counts.yaml` and buys a run that is exactly as writable as the one it replaces; the class (GATE-RUN-A) is untouched. **(c) is rejected:** a gate recorded red for a known, non-score-bearing cause is a muted gate — readers learn to read past red, which is the failure the gate exists to prevent (CE: no muted steps).
  1. **The allowance lives in the gate, not in `bench verify`.** `views.verify` and `bench verify row15-d1-1` keep exit 5 with both findings; only the loop at `check_regrade.py:118-122` consults the allowance. The archive's integrity record stays true.
  2. **Location and shape:** a sibling Leader-owned file `bench/regrade-allowed-findings.yaml`, not the 0.3 baseline (that file records exports; this records exceptions). Each entry carries `ruling: R-76`, `class: GATE-RUN-A`, `recorded: 2026-09-25`, and the register line it cites. Append-only: an entry is never edited or removed except when its run leaves the gate-run set (item 5).
  3. **Validity rule, mechanical:** an entry is accepted only if the first path segment under `ws/` is in `grade._changes.BUILD_OUTPUT` (`_changes.py:30`; `.git` is). Any entry outside that set is a gate failure at load time (`criterion 7: allowance names a graded path`), whatever the ruling id says.
  4. **The Inferred claim becomes a fixture.** "The pre-turn tree is read from objects, not the index" is Inferred from git semantics (the DR says so). Red-first: a test copies a gate cell's `ws/`, overwrites `.git/index` with junk bytes, and asserts `pre_turn_commit` returns the same commit and `pre_turn_tree` yields the same file-hash map as the untouched copy. Until it is green the allowance is not merged.
  5. **Expiry:** when `row15-d1-1` is replaced or a third gate run is added, its entries are deleted in the same commit; a new gate run starts with an empty allowance. An allowance may only ever shrink the set of runs it names, never grow it beyond this ruling's two tuples without a new ruling.
- **Reasoning:**
  - Both graders' inputs are proven, not recalled, to exclude the changed files: `grading_copy` copies with `ignore_patterns(*BUILD_OUTPUT)` (`_changes.py:77`) and `change_set` prunes `BUILD_OUTPUT` dirs (`:87`); the pre-turn side is `git archive <commit>` (`:59`), which item 4 makes Verified. The D1 values were re-measured under R-75 after the index change and are twice-equal (R-75 c3, `defect-classes.md:366`), which is the empirical half of the same claim.
  - Criterion 7's purpose is "the archive the gate reads is the archive that was captured". These two findings do not touch what the gate reads, so allowing them keeps the criterion's meaning; hiding them (in `bench verify`) or ignoring them (c) would not.
  - The gate's own ordering is a standing control: criterion 7 runs after passes A and B, so if grading ever wrote into the archive, a third finding appears and fails the gate. That is why the allowance is a closed list of tuples and not a code- or path-level waiver.
  - (a) carries no `assume:`; (b) rests on the belief that a fresh capture stays clean, which GATE-RUN-A's status `partially-controlled` (`:372`) already denies.
- **Conditions:**
  1. **Red-first in `tests/test_check_regrade.py`:** an allowed tuple is a note not a failure; the same tuple with a different cell, path or run is a failure; an entry whose path is outside `BUILD_OUTPUT` fails at load; an empty file passes; item 4's index-mangling fixture. All red before the allowance file lands.
  2. **Report header:** `bench report smoke-1` prints one line: `gate: row15-d1-1 criterion 7 — 2 verify errors allowed (R-76, GATE-RUN-A; ws/.git/index of 35af…, c3d4…; not read by any grader)`. The gate result is `pass (with allowance)`, never bare `pass`.
  3. **Register (CI):** the GATE-RUN-A entry gains the allowance as a control line ("an unrecoverable finding is pinned by tuple under a ruling; the gate fails on any other") and keeps status `partially-controlled`; the in-process `procs.run` cwd guard stays the named upgrade and is not displaced by this ruling.
  4. **Audit:** the commit that adds the file cites R-76 and DR-GATE-7; `catalog_hash` and the 0.4 freeze are untouched (no grader, rubric or metric moves).
  5. **Scope:** no other run, code or path is covered. A finding on `a1-capture-1`, or any `HB-` code other than `HB-LED-005` on these two cells, raises a new DR.

## R-77 · 2026-09-26 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-MUT-2: (a) the D1 red baseline is a property of the run, not the task — derived per run from its own Stryker cells, printed per run, flagged against the run's minimum; `bench/task-baselines.yaml` goes; (c) is a bounded follow-up, not a precondition

- **Ruling:** **(a).** R-75 c4 and c6 are amended: the baseline `initial_failing_tests` is **derived once per run**, never stored per task. For a run, the baseline is the **minimum** `initial_failing_tests` over the cells whose `mutation_score` was read from a Stryker report (NA cells excluded); the report header prints `D1 baseline red tests: <min>–<max> over <n> cells (derived from this run, R-77)`; a cell is flagged `red tests added` when its count exceeds **the run's minimum + flake band 1** (R-75 c6 otherwise unchanged: flagged, unscored, count beside the score). `bench/task-baselines.yaml` is **removed** — derive, don't store (DM): a stored 74–75 beside a measured 69 is two definitions of one quantity, and the report already proved which one a reader is handed. **(b) is rejected:** a band of 69–75 is a wrong number made to look right; it hides six added red tests on every run and is widened again at the next flap. **(c) is not a precondition:** smoke-1's header is wrong today, and the fix under (a) needs no knowledge of the cause; the cause is still owed (Conditions 4).
  1. **Fewer than two Stryker cells:** the baseline is `not derived (1 cell)`, the flag is `not checked`, printed as such — never `0`, never the task constant.
  2. **Spread disclosure:** when `max − min` exceeds the band inside one run, the header appends `baseline unstable within run (spread <max−min>)`. That is a finding for Conditions 4, not a flag on any cell.
  3. **The minimum-as-baseline belief is marked, not silent:** `assume: at least one Stryker cell in the run added no red test; confirmed by the TRX diff of Conditions 4; if false, the flag under-reports by the smallest added count, never over-reports.` It goes in the report code beside the derivation.
- **Reasoning:**
  - Verified on two runs of the same task hash and pack pin: 74 five times, twice each, and 69 five times, on the same grading copy shape. A quantity that is constant within a run and moves between runs is a property of the run's environment; recording it against the task version was the modelling error, and c4's "once per task" is the sentence that made it.
  - The within-run agreement (five equal cells, twice) is why the run's own cells are a usable reference and why the minimum is the right statistic: added red tests can only raise a cell's count above the shared environment floor.
  - Removing the file touches no grader, rubric or metric: the flag is unscored (R-75 c6) and the header is a report reader. No task hash, gate run or `catalog_hash` should move — checked at the join, not recalled (R-75 c3 shape).
  - (a) carries one marked assumption (item 3) with its confirmation named; (b) carries the unmarked belief that the next run lands inside 69–75; (c)-first leaves a run on disk whose disclosure is wrong for the duration of the investigation (IO: a measurement path degrades to `not recorded`, never to a plausible wrong number).
- **Conditions:**
  1. **Red-first:** report fixtures with cells `69,69,69,71` → one flagged, header `69–71 over 4 cells`, spread line present; `69` alone → `not derived (1 cell)`, no flag; `74,74,74,74,74` → no flag, no spread line; a fixture with the old task constant asserts no reader consults it; then `bench/task-baselines.yaml` is deleted in the same commit and `bench report smoke-1` prints `69–69 over 5 cells`.
  2. **Design edit** (W3-GR-CODE's owner): `phase3-graders.md` at the R-75 c2 lines replaces "recorded once per task" with the per-run derivation, cites R-77, and carries item 3's `assume:`.
  3. **`regression_count` sweep, before the follow-up runs:** whether its base run reads a vendored-suite result that can flap by the same tests is checked by opening the grader, not recalled; a finding raises a new DR, it does not widen this one.
  4. **Bounded follow-up (c):** one `dotnet test` of the vendored D1 suite in a grading copy with a TRX logger, twice (one under load, one idle), failing-test names diffed; the diff is written to `docs/notes/` and closes item 3's `assume:` either way. Cap: one worker, one deadline; a third run needs a ruling.
  5. **Class, not instance (CI):** `docs/lessons/defect-classes.md` gains "a baseline recorded as a constant of the artifact version when it is a property of the environment that produced it"; the control is that every disclosed baseline names the run it was derived from and fails closed to `not derived` (Conditions 1's fixtures). R-75 c5's sweep of `build_and_suite_clean` and `static_analysis_delta` is re-run for stored-baseline shapes.
  6. **Audit:** the commit cites R-77 and DR-MUT-2; R-75's text is not edited — this ruling amends it by reference.

## R-78 · 2026-09-27 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-S-1..6: every recommended default (a) is granted; anchors and weight corrections are catalog `0.5` content; statistics get their own export and golden; the seed is a recorded constant; E1–E3 are excluded from every pack-effect analysis

- **Ruling:**
  - **DR-S-1: (a).** Catalog `0.5` adds `anchor: [worst, best]` (raw units) to every metric with `kind: score` and `weight > 0`.
    - Under a catalog with no anchors, the composites are NA with the design's reason (`no normalisation anchor for <metric> in catalog <v>`), and the **primary measure is `pass_at_1` for the whole run**.
    - The header carries one line: `primary measure: pass@1 (catalog 0.4 has no normalisation anchors)`.
    - (c) is refused: spec `:212` says the anchors are catalog content, so an implicit range is a guess the catalog does not hold.
    - Rows stay ranked: the `0.4` smoke rank is a real result, not a placeholder.
  - **DR-S-2: (a).** `gated = pass_at_1 × overall` per cell, where `overall` is the unweighted mean of the non-NA area composites, with the design's three NA/0 cases (`:185-188`).
    - **Amendment:** `0.5` sets `pass_at_1` to `weight: 0`. It is the gate factor, and leaving it inside the correctness area counts the same quantity twice in one product. The design records this as a Decision.
    - The correctness area is then `partial_credit`, `build_and_suite_clean`, `mutation_score`, `regression_count`, `behavioural_equivalence`, `formal_checks_clean` and `bugs_confirmed`.
  - **DR-S-3: (a).** Cell composites read `kind: score` only. `0.5` sets `pass_hat_k`, `cost_of_pass`, `tokens_per_solved` and `wall_clock_split` to `weight: 0`. (b) is refused: it breaks the tasks × repetitions resampling unit and the R-19 per-task obligation.
  - **DR-S-4: (a).** The leaderboard leaves `views.export`, and `board.export` owns the statistics bytes and their own golden.
    - The change lands in the `0.5.dev` window (G3), and the Leader re-pins at the `0.5` freeze.
    - `views.export` under `0.4` keeps guarding scores; condition 4 makes the one-time equality explicit.
  - **DR-S-5: (a).** One named constant each: `stats.DEFAULT_SEED` and `stats.DEFAULT_RESAMPLES = 2000`, overridable by `--seed` and `--resamples`.
    - `seed`, `resamples` and `METHOD` are recorded in `board.export`, the CLI header and the HTML header (G12: one `disclosure_rows` source).
    - `bench report` refuses `--resamples < 2000` as invalid input. US-36 `:508` is an acceptance criterion, and G13 measured 0.024 s per interval at 2,000, so speed is no reason to go under.
    - The library function itself accepts any `B ≥ 1` for tests. No plan field.
  - **DR-S-6: (a).** US-52's comparison applies the same `CONTAMINATION_PRONE` exclusion and prints the same statement (`Excluded as contamination-prone: …` / `none in this run`): one constant, one reader path, one string.
- **Reasoning:**
  - Every default is the smallest option that keeps the spec's words true:
    - Anchors live in the catalog (`:212`), so they arrive with a version, not with code.
    - The seed is a stored input (`:255`), and the smallest store is a recorded constant.
    - `views.export` is the US-4 guard on scores under a catalog version. Statistics code is not catalog content; coupling the two makes every bootstrap fix look like a silent score move, the very defect US-4 detects.
  - DR-S-2's amendment and DR-S-3 share one principle: the catalog states what the code does, and the code enforces it (CI6: a weight the code ignores is a memoir).
  - The `0.4` smoke result stays honest under DR-S-1(a). Its rank is on pass@1, as the header says, and the `0.5` re-grade is pure and cached (US-26), so nothing is thrown away.
  - E1–E3 are `stub` today (G20), so DR-S-6 costs the smoke run one line: `none in this run`.
  - R4 closes: S4–S7 may dispatch.
- **Conditions:**
  1. **`0.5.dev` must contain:**
     - `version: "0.5.dev"`;
     - `anchor: [worst, best]` on every `kind: score, weight > 0` metric, each with a one-clause `note` giving the anchor's source: a spec value, a measured range or a stated convention, never unmarked;
     - `weight: 0` on `pass_at_1` and on the four DR-S-3 metrics;
     - no other change to metric ids, kinds, `better`, `grader` or `scale`.
  2. `config.validate_metrics` (G7 accepts unknown keys today) gains three refusals, red first: a weighted `kind: score` metric without an anchor; `worst == best`; and an anchor whose direction contradicts `better`. A `kind: derived` metric with `weight > 0` is also refused. S4 owns these as a seam to `config.py`.
  3. Every header names the primary measure: `pass@1` while the current pass's catalog has no anchors, `gated` otherwise. It is a run-level property, never per row (design `:270-271`; T-U1..U3 pin the string).
  4. Before `board.export` lands, the Leader records in the run record that the `0.4` golden `heads.export`, minus its `leaderboard` key, is byte-equal to `views.export` of the `0.4` fixture after the change. This is one check, done once; the scores stay guarded under `0.4` without a second golden.
  5. **Freeze `0.5` only when** all of these hold:
     - S4–S7 have joined;
     - the `board.export` and `views.export` goldens are pinned for the fixtures graded under `0.5`;
     - the frozen-fixture check is green;
     - `smoke-1` has a current pass under `0.5` whose header reads `primary measure: gated`;
     - the four `weight: 0` corrections and every anchor are in;
     - `bench/catalog-freeze.yaml` holds the `0.5` hash and both goldens.
     A change after that freeze is `0.6` (R-59 c3's rule).
  6. `stats.CONTAMINATION_PRONE` is the only source of the exclusion. T-M1..M3 include one case where run B holds an `E*` task and the comparison section names it under the same statement as US-37's.
  7. `--resamples < 2000` in `bench report` exits invalid-input with a named code in the `HB-STA` family. The design's error table gains that row before S6 closes.
  8. The smoke run's rank under `0.4` is reported as a pass@1 rank in any operator-facing summary. The AI summary (US-42) may not describe it as a composite rank.

## R-79 · 2026-09-27 · Owner seat (Fable) · Catalog 0.5.dev anchor review: APPROVE WITH CHANGES; measured anchors saturate, never shift other cells; judged metrics anchored to their rubric's shape; DR-C1 (a)

- **Ruling:** the draft on `w4-cat05` (commits `6f61db8`, `e0726cc`) is approved with the changes below. The weight-0 corrections are Verified exact against R-78 condition 1, and nothing else moved.
  1. **Normalisation saturates** (design `phase4-statistics.md:163-167`, Verified): `t=(x−worst)/(best−worst)`, `N=100×clamp(t,0,1)`. A later value past an anchor scores 0 or 100 and never reads another cell. The catalog comment states this rule and the three `anchor_note` forms (`spec <path>:<line>` | `measured <run> <min>..<max> (n tasks, k runs)` | `convention: <reason>`). A measured or provisional anchor is re-anchored in the next version when a run saturates a cell.
  2. **Replacements:**
     - `cache_write_amplification` → `[100.0000, 0.0000]`, from spec `docs/design/phase3-cost.md:116`.
     - `time_to_first_green` → `[3600000, 0]`, from spec `docs/specs/harness-bench.md:266` (the 60-minute task ceiling).
     - `regression_count` → `[1, 0]`, by convention: one regression is a correctness failure (design `phase3-graders.md:331`).
     - The seven judged metrics without a rubric (`honest_completion_claims`, `error_handling`, `assumption_disclosure`, `handoff_fidelity` → `[0, 2]`; `goal_drift_slope`, `unrequested_behaviour`, `mast_failure_codes` → `[2, 0]`), each noted as "judged sum of n items at 0..2 (judge.py:22, adr_quality.md:5), n=1 provisional until the rubric lands; re-anchor to 2n at 0.6".
  3. **Notes reworded** (anchors kept):
     - `context_growth`, `scope_creep`, `stuck_loops`: measured, worst = 2× the smoke-1 max, re-anchor at 0.6 if any cell saturates.
     - `cost_usd`: $10 is provisional until the first priced run.
     - `static_analysis_delta`: no spec ceiling; a negative delta clamps to 100.
     - `constraint_violations`: provisional until the first checklist exists.
     - `coordination_overhead`: undefined in code today.
     - `convention_drift`: the unit comes from design `phase3-graders.md:426`; the draft cited the wrong line (`:128`).
  4. **DR-C1: (a).** S4 adds two refusals to `bench validate`. A judged metric whose `|best − worst| != 2 × rubric item count` is refused whenever a `rubrics:` entry exists. A `better: lower` judged rubric must define 2 as the worst score per item.
- **Reasoning:**
  - Saturation removes the fear that one smoke run's range moves the whole board. What remains is resolution loss and one-run bias, which the re-anchor rule bounds.
  - Judged metrics are scored as the sum of 0/1/2 per rubric item (`judge.py:22-24`, Verified), so their anchor is structural: `[0, 2n]`. The draft's shapes contradicted the code.
- **Conditions:**
  1. The applied diff is minimal: anchors and notes are added to the existing metric lines without re-flowing unrelated lines, so review reads only the change.
  2. Tests that hard-code catalog `"0.4"` read the catalog's own version (or `make_root`'s release label) instead. The stale gate stamp is renewed by the Leader with a real `pytest -m gate` run before the merge.
- **Findings for the Leader (not catalog changes):**
  - A smoke-1 `context_growth` single-call peak of 2.48M tokens exceeds any served context window. `cost.py:161` sums uncached + cache_read + cache_write per call; the grader needs a check (Inferred).
  - Judged metrics other than `adr_quality` carry no `scale` while `judge.py:50` writes scale-1 decimals. `scale` is frozen by R-78 condition 1, so this is recorded for 0.6.

## R-80 · 2026-09-28 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-EG-1..3: (a) report-record.json is the publication record and `egress_events` is retired; the canary source is one production constant set; the operator's email is optional, username and home are OS facts, and the section scan always runs

- **Ruling:**
  1. **DR-EG-1: (a).** `report-record.json` beside `report.html` is the record of the publication scan: a derived artifact of `bench report`, regenerated with the report, never a ledger fact.
     - Judge-call withholdings are already ledger facts and need nothing new. A hit is `verdict_uses` outcome `failed`, code `HB-GW-009` (`gateway/pipeline.py:168`, Verified), and the score is null with the reason `judge <model>: failed HB-GW-009` (`grade/judge.py:200`, Verified).
     - ADR-0006's `egress_events` row is a declared fact with no writer (no `src/` file names it, Verified). Amend ADR-0006 to retire the row and to state the two records that stand in its place: on the judge side, `verdict_uses.outcome/code` plus `model_calls` principal `gateway`; on the publication side, `report-record.json`.
     - No migration: no row was ever written. The `assume:` in `html.write` closes on this ruling.
  2. **DR-EG-2: finding first — no production canary exists.**
     - The US-13 canaries are test values: Copilot's deterministic class strings in `tests/e2e/test_us13_canary.py` (`HB-US13-*`). The Claude Code and Codex controls copy the operator's *real* user files, which are not enumerable values; the operator-identifier classes cover them.
     - US-48 is withdrawn for authored tasks (ADR-0013); its Harbor canaries are phase 2.
     - **The source the scan must read is one production constant set, `egress.CANARIES`**, holding the US-13 class strings. The test module imports it (one definition, DM7).
     - `_judge_calls` and `cmd_report` pass it unconditionally, so `scanned` always names `canary`.
     - There is no per-run generated canary and no plan or ledger field for one. Nothing plants a canary in a production cell, and a stored value would be a second copy of a test fixture.
  3. **DR-EG-3: (c), with the email optional and disclosed.**
     - `Operator.email` becomes `str | None`. Username and home come from the OS, as `cli.py:236` already does.
     - Without `BENCH_OPERATOR_EMAIL`, `bench report` still runs every other class. `scanned` omits `email`, the record's `egress` reads `partial: email not supplied`, and the report header's egress row says the same.
     - `bench grade --allow-model-calls` keeps its hard refusal (`HB-USR-002`): a judge payload leaves the machine, whereas a report is a local file until the operator publishes it.
- **Reasoning:**
  - One quantity, one definition. A judge withholding already has a durable, hash-chained home with a closed enum and a reason on the score. A second `egress_events` row would be the drift ADR-0006 rejected (DM7). A report spans up to two runs and is rebuilt on every `bench report`, so its scan record belongs beside the artifact it describes, bound by digest.
  - A caller-supplied canary list with no production caller is a scan that never runs while its class name suggests it might. A constant set costs nothing and makes `scanned` honest.
  - **Finding, larger than DR-EG-3 states:** with `operator is None`, `_publish` returns the page unscanned (`report/html.py:463-464`, Verified). There is no section scan at all, no credential values and no canaries; only the HB-SEC-001 shape pass follows. Option (a) as it stands would keep that gap. Under (c) the section scan always runs, and `Verdict.scanned` keeps R-60 c4's invariant that a clean result never reads like "not scanned".
- **Conditions:**
  1. `report-record.json` gains `report_sha256`, the digest of the written `report.html`, so the pair is bound. The report header names the record path and the withheld count. Red first: a test reading the record's digest against the file.
  2. The ADR-0006 amendment is reviewed by the Data & Persistence Architect before merge. `docs/design/phase3-gateway-judges.md:324`, `:335`, `:733` and `:962` follow it in the same change.
  3. DR-EG-2, red first:
     - `_judge_calls(...)` returns `Calls.canaries == egress.CANARIES`;
     - `bench report` on a fixture run yields a record where every section's `scanned` contains `canary`.
     Both fail today, because both call sites pass `()` (`cli.py:249`, `:325`, Verified). The record's `canaries` field names the set's version and `us48: not planted (Harbor, phase 2)`.
  4. DR-EG-3, red first: `bench report` without the environment variable yields per-section verdicts, `scanned` without `email`, and `egress: partial: email not supplied`. The `operator is None` early return in `_publish` is deleted. `Operator.__post_init__` requires a non-empty username and home; the email is non-empty or `None`.
  5. Mutants for each new branch join `tests/mutations/egress.json`. The Leader stamps the gate with a real run.
  6. No live test is required for this ruling; US-46 c2 and US-47 c3 stand as run today.

## R-81 · 2026-09-28 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-R-1..10: DR-R-1..9 every recommended default is granted; DR-R-10 (b): board goldens are keyed by `board.EXPORT_VERSION`, the catalog stays `0.5`, and the US-4 control checks the (catalog, export-version) pair

- **Ruling:**
  - **DR-R-1: granted.** Heatmap text is `#000` or `#fff` (`--on-heat-*`), whichever contrasts more. The spec's clause "switches ink by cell luminance to keep 4.5:1" (`:1009`) stays true as written; only the ink tokens differ. The bound is Verified by the design's arithmetic: the two ratios multiply to 21, so the larger is at least √21 = 4.58.
  - **DR-R-2: granted.** Archive presence is decided at generation time, and the pointer text is always visible. The residual risk, that a file copied alone shows a dead link, is accepted and stated in About this run.
  - **DR-R-3: granted.** Dark mode follows `prefers-color-scheme`, with no toggle and no stored preference.
  - **DR-R-4: granted.** Per combo: the `pack=on` cells whose pass@1 differs from their paired `pack=off` cell, at most 2 per combo, chosen by cell id; up to 8,000 characters each; each excerpt passes through `egress.check`.
  - **DR-R-5: granted.** `bench report <run> --summaries` is the only trigger, and it refuses while any run is live. Plain `bench report` makes no model call.
  - **DR-R-6: granted, composed with R-80.** `egress.check` runs per embedded excerpt and per summary, a hit withholds that item as `withheld: sensitive content`, and HB-SEC-001 stays the page-level backstop. The canary source is `egress.CANARIES` (R-80 c3), the scan always runs (R-80 c4), and the verdicts land in `report-record.json` (R-80 c1).
  - **DR-R-7: granted.** F-1 is fixed in R0 under row 20, with the reason string from phase4-statistics `:336`. No live seam: S5 has joined (the `0.5` freeze entry carries `board_golden`, which R-78 c5 allowed only after S4–S7 joined). The class is registered at the fix (condition 4).
  - **DR-R-8: granted.** The section and nav label is **Runs**; the table caption is "Every cell of the run".
  - **DR-R-9: granted.** `playwright` becomes a pinned dev dependency, with a vendored, pinned `axe-core` under `tests/vendor/` and its MPL-2.0 licence, in a `browser` pytest marker ring run at readiness.
  - **DR-R-10: (b).**
    - `board.py` gains `EXPORT_VERSION: int`. The `0.5` freeze pinned version `1`; R0 ships `2`.
    - The export bytes carry `"export_version"` beside `method` and `catalog_version`, and the header's disclosure row names it (G12, one source).
    - Board goldens are pinned per export version, and the catalog stays `0.5`.
    - R-78 c5's last sentence is amended: *a catalog-content change after the freeze is `0.6`; a statistics-code change is `EXPORT_VERSION + 1`, and both are visible in the bytes.*
    - (a) is refused: a `0.6` whose `catalog_hash` equals `0.5`'s says content changed when it did not (ADR-0006:59, content-addressed).
    - (c) is refused: check (e) is append-only against the merge base precisely so that a same-commit re-pin cannot pass. An audited exception is the control's own defect signature.
  - **The freeze record.**
    - A new top-level, append-only `board_exports:` map is keyed by export version.
    - Its first entry is `'1': {catalog: '0.5', golden: {c44dd2b-no-heads: 0f745f…, heads: 0f745f…}}`. It is a copy of the freeze-time pin, which the control holds equal, as R-59 DR-5 holds the rubric copy.
    - Then `'2': {catalog: '0.5', golden: {…}}` is written at R0's join.
    - The existing `'0.5'.board_golden` is untouched. It remains the freeze's historical record, still guarded by (c) and (e).
  - **The control (`tests/test_catalog_version.py`).** Board goldens live at `tests/fixtures/board/<N>/<fixture>.board.export`; the `0.5` tree's `.board.export` files stay frozen. For a released catalog `v` and the current `N`:
    - (a) the `board.export` of each fixture graded under `v` equals its golden under `N`;
    - (c) the golden files' sha256 equal `board_exports[N].golden`;
    - (d) fails when `board_exports[N]` is absent or its `catalog` is not `v`;
    - (e) extends to `board_exports`: an entry present at the merge base is never changed or removed. The control also asserts `board_exports['1'].golden == versions['0.5'].board_golden`.
    The `.dev` exemption is unchanged. A board byte change without an `EXPORT_VERSION` bump fails (a) under a released catalog, as today. A bump without a new `board_exports` entry fails (d).
- **Reasoning:**
  - DR-R-1..9 are each the smallest option that keeps the spec's words true. The two vetoes (Test Architect, UX & Accessibility) already passed the design with these defaults in it (`phase4-report.md` §17).
  - DR-R-10 carries DR-S-4's intent one step further. R-78 separated statistics bytes from catalog bytes so that "a statistics fix never looks like a silent score move". Keying the board golden by catalog version re-coupled them the moment the first board fix arrived. Two independently versioned things need two version numbers, each in the bytes it governs.
  - R0 does not touch `views.export`, and the control's views check (a) proves it at the join. If the F-1 fix moved a score, (a) fails on `heads.export`, and the answer is a catalog bump after all.
  - The past record keeps its meaning. A `0.5` pass rendered under export `1` reads every area NA and says `export_version: 1`; the same pass under `2` says `2`. Nothing is rewritten (DM: Type-2 by version, derive don't store).
- **Conditions:**
  1. **DR-R-10 lands red first, in R0, before any other board change:** first the control's new (a)/(c)/(d)/(e) cases on the 9.x fixture root (`test_catalog_version.py:166`), then `EXPORT_VERSION = 2`, then the `board_exports` rows. R0's join reports the control's full output, not just its exit code. `bench/catalog-freeze.yaml` stays Leader-owned.
  2. A change to `METHOD`, any export key, any encoding (precision, `null` for missing) or any row or column addition bumps `EXPORT_VERSION`. The comment on the constant says so, and T-B3's pinned digest comment (`phase4-statistics.md:681`) is updated to name `EXPORT_VERSION` too.
  3. DR-R-4/5: the summary manifest records the sampling rule's name, the cell ids chosen and each excerpt's length. The live-run refusal exits with a named `HB-` code, added to the design's error table before R7 closes.
  4. DR-R-7: `docs/lessons/defect-classes.md` gains "code diverging from its own design's reason text". Its control is a test asserting that the one-arm reason equals the literal `not computed (no <area> score in pack=<arm>)`.
  5. DR-R-6: each withheld excerpt is a row in `report-record.json` (section, item id, class), and the header's withheld count includes it (R-80 c1).
  6. DR-R-9: the Security & Identity Architect reviews both new dependencies before merge (a new dependency convenes that lens). The `axe-core` file's sha256 is recorded beside its licence. `playwright install` runs only in the readiness ring, never in the offline gate. The `browser` ring is a fail-closed required check at readiness.
  7. The audit entry for R0 cites this ruling and the amended R-78 c5 sentence.

## R-82 · 2026-09-29 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-W5-1..2: both rows were retired by R-7 five days ago and the retirement never landed; B3 is (b) on the cfd-bench P1 paragraph, C2 is (c) on ai-de, and BOM 0.4 records both

- **Ruling:**
  - **Finding first, larger than either request.** R-7 (2026-09-24) already ruled B3 and C2 **operator-authored substitutes**, for the same two reasons the workers found again:
    - SpecBench's only URL is unreachable (R-7 row 16).
    - ArchBench ships no requirement-to-architecture task and is API-keyed (R-7 rows 18, 27).

    R-7 c2 and c4 ordered `bench/bom.yaml` to `source: authored`, with a dated note. Neither change landed: `bom.yaml:26` and `:29` still read `SpecBench` and `ArchBench`, and `tasks/B3/task.yaml` and `tasks/C2/task.yaml` are stubs that cite them with `repo: tbd` (Verified 2026-09-29). Two workers spent 50 minutes re-deriving a closed ruling. Condition 5 registers the class.
  - **DR-W5-1: (b), as R-7 c2 ruled, with the source amended to cfd-bench.**
    - B3 is a scenario-2 primer-to-spec task on the **P1 phase paragraph** of `docs/proposals/build-phasing-plan.html`, at B1's pin `496a0a8`, with the same vendored file and the same licence record. Its target is C#/.NET 10, as for every authored non-G task.
    - It is graded as B2 is: structural checks, at least two decided-content checks, and the judge against a reference spec.
    - R-7 c2 named `ai-forward ca032f0` as the source. That is released: B1's pin and vendoring already exist, and ai-forward keeps G1/G2.
    - (a) is refused. The repository has never been crawled, and "results first" does not wait on a paper's promise.
    - (c) is refused. It changes scenario 2's shape, and its only oracle is a paid two-judge ensemble, which subscriptions-only excludes.
  - **DR-W5-2: (c), as R-7 c4 ruled.**
    - (a) is refused on three verified grounds:
      - R2ABench's public repository (`github.com/luuquangkhai9/R2ABench`, `89c43b5`) has **no licence** (`license: null`, and no `LICENSE` in the tree). Its 68 requirements documents and reference views therefore cannot be vendored into a public bench repository.
      - Both Figshare links (`/s/01f0a5…` from the arXiv page, `/s/3ef188…` from the README) are private share links and return **403**.
      - Its L2 judge reads `OPENAI_API_KEY` and defaults to a third-party relay (`l2_semantic.py:25,41`). Its L0 needs a Java PlantUML install, a third toolchain for one task.
    - (b) is refused. A serverless component task is scenario 5, not 3, and its optional judge is keyed.
    - (d) is refused. Scenario 3 would hold one task.
    - C2's source is **ai-de**, not cfd-bench, because the proposal's design rules defer "architecture-from-spec on cfd-bench P0–P1" to BOM v1.
    - Default input: B2's reference spec (`tasks/B2/oracle/reference/docs/specs/freshness-prober.md`) is the given spec, so scenario 3 consumes what scenario 2 produces. The Leader may instead pick another `docs/architecture.md` capability at pin `88e0c33`.
    - It is graded like C1: structural checks on `docs/architecture.md`, plus the judge rubric against a reference architecture note. R2ABench's three-layer grading is cited by name as the pattern; no text, rubric or data is copied.
  - **The inventory is corrected once, not per task.**
    - `bench/bom.yaml` becomes **0.4**. B3 and C2 get `source: authored`, with the notes R-7 c2/c4 specified, dated 2026-09-24 and re-verified 2026-09-29. C2's note names R2ABench (arXiv 2604.06683) as the paper the row described, and says why it is not used.
    - The proposal's rows 119 and 121, and the two public-inventory rows (`:99`, `:100`), say the same in the same commit.
- **Reasoning:**
  - A ruling whose file conditions never land is a ruling that did not happen. The workers stopped correctly; the defect is upstream of them. Ruling the same thing a second time is only worth it if the register learns the class, so that no third time is needed.
  - Both substitutes are the proposal's own fallback ("borrow the grading pattern; tasks are ours", R-7 reasoning), so nothing here is new design. The licence and key facts on R2ABench are the Owner's constraints applied, not a judgement of the paper's quality.
  - Reusing B1's source pin and B2's reference spec is the Solution-Selection Ladder's reuse rung: no new licence check, no new vendoring decision, no new toolchain.
- **Conditions:**
  1. **B3, red first:**
     - `prompt.md` gives the P1 paragraph and the spec template sections.
     - The hidden checks are structural, plus at least two decided-content checks. Each check has a control under `oracle/reference/controls/` that fails exactly its own check (A5's pattern).
     - `oracle/reference/docs/specs/` holds the reference spec. `rubric.md` is the /specify definition of done. `evidence.md` records fail-on-base and pass-on-reference. `bench validate` is ok.
     - `task.yaml`: `source.kind: authored`, B1's `repo`/`commit`/`license` lines verbatim, `language: csharp`, `budget.minutes: 20`.
  2. **C2, red first:**
     - The given is the spec named above, verbatim.
     - The hidden checks require `docs/architecture.md` with the sections C1 requires, plus a component list and one ADR-style decision with alternatives. There are at least two decided-content checks, each with a control as in c1.
     - `oracle/reference/` holds the reference architecture note. `rubric.md` cites R2ABench's L1/L2 layers by name only. `evidence.md` is present. `bench validate` is ok.
     - `task.yaml`: `source.kind: authored`, ai-de's MIT lines from B2 verbatim, `language: csharp`, `budget.minutes: 30`.
  3. BOM 0.4 and the proposal corrections land in one Leader commit **before** either worker's join, so each join is made against a row that names its real source.
  4. Each worker's report names the wall clock spent re-verifying, so the cost of the unlanded ruling is measured, not estimated.
  5. `docs/lessons/defect-classes.md` gains "a ruling's file condition closed without landing, so a later wave re-derived it". The control is the Leader's choice. At minimum, the wave join for any ruling with file conditions lists each named file with the commit that changed it, and a join that cannot name the commit is red. R-7 c2/c4 are the first entries.
  6. No live run is required for this ruling.

## R-83 · 2026-09-29 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-W5-3: E1–E3 are (a), selected under ADR-0013 Amendment 1's own selection rule from one committed survey of all 89 TB2 tasks; a shortfall relaxes the band before it defers, and is never authored under an E1–E3 id; (d) no engine change, the limitation goes in the task contract

- **Ruling:**
  - **What R-7 already decided, and what stands.**
    - R-7 row 21 ruled TB2 *reachable as is, gated by row 9*. R-7 c1 pins `source.commit` to a real commit. R-7 c5 and R-40 deferred E1 from the smoke run and made E6 the scenario-5 smoke task: "not substituted; the E-tasks are the calibration baseline".
    - ADR-0013 Amendment 1 (2026-09-28) then withdrew the row-9 gate. It also ruled the selection rule that the Leader's option (a) restates. A public task gets a pinned upstream instance, a native working copy, a toolchain installed by the task's own setup, and its upstream tests on the host. An instance whose setup or tests need Linux, or only one of Windows and macOS, is not selected, and "its task picks another from the same upstream band and records why" (`0013:81-84`).
    - So the selection rule is not new. What is new: the survey's scope and stop rule, what a shortfall does, the band assignment, and the reflog limitation.
  - **DR-W5-3: (a), bounded to one committed survey.**
    - One worker reads every TB2 task's `task.toml` and `environment/Dockerfile` at `2fd12b88` (89 folders; no task is run to survey it). It writes `docs/notes/tb2-native-survey.md`: one row per task, with its upstream difficulty, its apt lines, a verdict (`native` / `apt` / `linux-only` / `git-state`) and the reason.
    - The E1 worker's four easy-band rows (audit `al-01M3Q61B6QFAQP08P0BKZFVRW9`) are the survey's first four rows, cited, not re-derived.
    - The survey is committed before any task folder changes, so no later wave derives it a third time (R-82 c5's class).
    - `native` means all of these hold:
      - the Dockerfile installs only `uv`-managed Python or Node beyond the base image;
      - the tests are `tests/test_outputs.py` (pytest) or a portable equivalent;
      - the task's mechanic is a tree (see (d) below);
      - no step is Linux-only.
    - Verdicts come from reading. The `ready` gate is the run on this host (R-7 c6's pattern).
    - E1, E2 and E3 take the first `native` task of the easy, medium and hard bands respectively, by the upstream's own difficulty field, verbatim.
    - **Shortfall, in this order:**
      1. A band with no `native` task takes a `native` task from the nearest band, and the BOM title records the actual band.
      2. If TB2 has fewer than three `native` tasks in total, the unfilled row stays `stub`, `smoke: false`, with a dated BOM note, as R-40 did for E1.
    - **(b) is refused as the shortfall.** E1–E3 are the calibration baseline (proposal `:41`, `:94`; R-7 c5). They are also the contamination-prone set that `CONTAMINATION_PRONE` (`board.py:26`), US-51 (`spec:512`) and `tasks/README.md:36` exclude from the pack effect by id. An authored task under one of those ids is neither a calibration point nor contamination-prone, so the title, the constant and the exclusion statement would all become false. If the Leader wants an authored scenario-5 task, it gets a new row id in a BOM version bump, outside that set.
    - **(c) is refused.** Amendment 1 closed the container question; there is nothing to revisit.
  - **(d): no engine change for BOM v0.**
    - The engine models a task as a base tree at one commit. `task_source()` copies `tasks/<ID>/workspace/`, runs `git init` and makes one commit (`workspace.py:93-105`). The cell copy is `git clone --local` (`:110-117`).
