    - Git state (history, reflog, dangling objects) therefore never enters a task in the first place. Preserving the reflog in the clone would not make fix-git run.
    - A repository-state task kind is an ADR-0013 change, not a wave-5 row.
    - The limitation is recorded in one sentence in the task contract, `tasks/README.md`: *a task is a tree, not a repository; a task whose mechanic depends on git history, reflog or dangling objects is not selectable (R-83).* No ADR amendment: the ADR already says "base commit".
- **Reasoning:**
  - Amendment 1 already contains the selection rule and the same-band fallback. This ruling only bounds the survey so that it happens once, and says what "no task in the band" means, which Amendment 1 does not.
  - Keeping the E ids public keeps three things consistent that are coupled by id in the code, the spec and the contract. Relaxing the band costs one word in a title. Authoring under the id costs the meaning of the calibration and of the exclusion.
  - A survey of 89 `task.toml` and Dockerfile reads is smaller than one authored task with tests and a reference, and it answers the question exactly. Four of 89 read so far is not evidence about the other 85.
  - The reflog finding is correct, but the fix is misplaced: the history is lost one layer above the clone. Naming the class in the contract is the control that keeps a future worker from selecting the next fix-git.
- **Conditions:**
  1. The survey lands as one commit. Its header gives the git command used to enumerate the 89 folders and the full SHA of `2fd12b88`. A verdict of `native` cites the Dockerfile lines it read.
  2. Each selected task folder follows E6's pattern:
     - `task.yaml` has `source.kind: public`, `repo: https://github.com/harbor-framework/terminal-bench-2`, the full commit SHA and `license: Apache-2.0`;
     - the upstream `LICENSE` is copied into the folder;
     - the vendored paths are listed, so a test can rebuild them from `git archive <commit> -- <paths>` (R-42 c3);
     - `instruction.md` becomes `prompt.md`, and any edit for native paths is recorded as a diff in the folder's README;
     - `tests/test_outputs.py` goes under `tests/` and `solution/solve.sh` under `oracle/reference/`; neither enters `workspace/` (US-8).
  3. `ready` needs the upstream tests run on this host: fail on the base tree, pass on the reference, and `bench validate` ok. The `dotnet`/`uv`/`node` version is recorded in `evidence.md`. macOS is recorded as unverified until the engine's macOS port (ADR-0013 §5) exists; that is not a reason to skip a task.
  4. The `assume:` on the 60-minute budget (`bom.yaml:38`) closes for each row by recording the task's own `task.toml` agent timeout beside it. The BOM budget stays 60 unless the timeout is smaller.
  5. `bench/bom.yaml` becomes **0.5**. E1–E3 rows carry the selected task's upstream name and actual band; an unfilled row carries "no native TB2 task in band (R-83, survey <commit>)". E1's `task.yaml` values `repo: harbor:terminal-bench@2.0` and `commit: tbd` are replaced in the same commit.
  6. `tasks/README.md` gains the one-sentence tree-not-repository rule above, with fix-git as its first cited instance. The wave join lists this file and the BOM with the commit that changed each (R-82 c5).
  7. No live run is required for this ruling.

## R-84 · 2026-09-29 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-FM1..2 and the G2-first sequence: DR-FM1 is a spike with exit criteria, not a mechanism ruled from memory; DR-FM2 is ruled from the proposal's own rule (no failing test → an unconfirmed claim), with only the judge-pending branch excluded from N; G2 lands first, the spike runs beside it, and a stub never scores a real pass

- **Ruling:**
  - **DR-FM1: the spike is granted; the mechanism is not.**
    - The design's recommended default names "TLC's documented trace-validation feature, a generated `<Model>TTrace.tla`/`.cfg` pair". Its source is a community wiki that nobody in this repo has opened (`formal-grader.md:132`, which its own author labels Flagged/Inferred).
    - *Inferred, not verified:* the `*TTrace*.tla` file that TLC emits is the replay artifact for a counterexample TLC found itself. Validating an *externally recorded* trace is a different pattern: a trace spec that constrains `Next` to the recorded events. Which pattern applies is the spike's first question, not a premise to build on.
    - The Python-side symbolic replay alternative is refused. A second TLA+ evaluator would be two definitions of one quantity (DM), and the toolchain is already pinned.
    - The spike is one worker task, `docs/notes/spike-fm1-tla-trace-validation.md`, in S-12's shape. Its exit evidence, all measured on this host:
      1. The S-12 scaffold or the `tests/fixtures/grade/formal/g1/` reference model **accepts** one recorded real-fold trace and **rejects** one bug-seeded trace. Accept or reject is read from a closed predicate on TLC's output (the exit code plus a named line), never from "no error printed".
      2. On rejection, a line in the evidence log identifies the first divergent step (the `<path>:<line>` convention, `formal-grader.md:274`).
      3. The two named golden-master tests (`test_g1_reference_cell_all_scores`, `test_g1_seeded_variant_cell_fails_non_vacuity`) pass against the real `_replay`, not the stub.
      4. The **trace interface** is written down: the state variables and action names the generated trace spec binds to. Without it, an *agent-authored* model cannot be trace-validated. G1's `prompt.md` states the interface as a task rule. A model that does not expose it accepts no trace and scores `model_conformance = 0.0000`, not NA: failing to follow the prompt is the agent's failure, and NA is only for what cannot be measured.
    - These constraints hold whatever the spike finds:
      - `tla2tools.jar` is the only jar, unless a second one is needed (CommunityModules, if the trace spec needs `Json`/`IOUtils`). A second jar is pinned by sha256 and warmed before the clock, exactly as the first is (`formal-grader.md:214`).
      - The generated trace spec and `.cfg` are written by the grader into `out_dir`, never into the archive.
      - The recorded traces stay hidden oracle assets (US-3).
      - `cwd` is the `spec/` directory (G8).
      - The spike runs natively on Windows now. macOS is recorded as unverified (FM11), which is not a reason to wait.
  - **DR-FM2: ruled from the proposal's own rule, not the recommended default.**
    - Proposal `:140`: *"Every bug a G-task agent reports must come with a failing test on the real code, or it counts as an unconfirmed claim."* US-33 `:472`: precision = confirmed ÷ N, where N is the number reported.
    - The decision tree is fully mechanical. It needs no "does it match the seed" step:

      | Entry outcome | In N | Confirmed |
      | --- | --- | --- |
      | no `Test:` line, or the reference fails validation (`..`, a leading `-`, outside `tests/**`), so it is never run | yes | no |
      | the test **passes** on the real fold | yes | no (refuted) |
      | fails on the real fold, **passes** on the reference fix | yes | yes (seeded branch) |
      | fails on the real fold, **fails** on the fix | **excluded** | — (judge-pending; disclosed) |

    - Only the last row is the not-seeded judge branch that US-33 names, and only that row is excluded. This mirrors R-68's NA-by-design rule: the mechanical rung has done everything it can, and the judge rubric does not exist.
    - The design's exclusion of unvalidated and no-`Test:` entries (`formal-grader.md:204`) is **refused**. A reported bug with no runnable test is the proposal's "unconfirmed claim", and the prompt states the format, so the format is part of the task.
    - Two fixtures move: "one correct plus one path-traversal `Test:` line" and "one correct plus one no-`Test:` entry" now read `0.5000`, not `1.0000`.
    - The Security & Identity Architect's control is untouched: the entry is still never passed to `run_step`.
    - `BUGS.md` schema: the recommended minimum is granted. There is one `## <slug>` per claim and one `Test: tests/<file>.py::<function>` line. The schema lives in `tasks/README.md`'s scenario-7 contract and verbatim in G1's `prompt.md`. The reference solution's own `BUGS.md` proves it can be satisfied.
    - No `BUGS.md`, or one with no entries: `bugs_confirmed = 0`, because the seeded bug exists and was not found, which is a measurement. `bug_claim_precision` is NA `0/0`. The design's NA `no BUGS.md` for `bugs_confirmed` is refused: NA is never 0, and 0 is never NA.
    - When the judge rubric is written, it is a catalog bump (US-4), and `0.5` rows keep their values. Until then, the judge-pending count is a line in the evidence log, not a metric.
  - **Sequence: granted.**
    - G2 (Lean) is designed, authored and implemented first.
    - The DR-FM1 spike runs beside it as an independent worker.
    - G1's conformance and non-vacuity slice starts only on the spike's exit evidence.
    - Every G2 metric can be computed without `_replay` (`model_non_vacuity` on G2 is a second `lake build`, `formal-grader.md:189`), so one formal task can reach `ready` on this evidence alone.
- **Reasoning:**
  - "Results first" is served by the sequence, not by shortening the spike. G2 is a real result. A G1 conformance score from a mechanism nobody ran would be a plausible wrong number (IO).
  - Ruling DR-FM1's mechanism now would repeat the design's own mistake: asserting a documented feature from memory. The exit criteria are what US-32 `:465` actually demands; the mechanism is whatever meets them.
  - DR-FM2's default penalised nothing that could not be judged. That is right for the judge-pending row and wrong for the rows the mechanical rung already decides. A claim with no failing test is exactly the case the proposal wrote the rule for.
  - The trace interface is the load-bearing finding: without it, G1 cannot be authored, whatever the spike says about TLC.
- **Conditions:**
  1. The `_replay` stub is test-only. In a real grading pass, G1's `model_conformance` and `model_non_vacuity` read NA `not built` until the spike's items 1–3 are committed. When `formal` registers, `test_grade_runner.py:73-77` keeps that shape for those two ids.
  2. G2 `ready` needs all of the following:
     - `formal.theorem_names` in `task.yaml`;
     - US-2 `:271`'s third clause met in `evidence.md`, with the buggy fold as the seeded bug and the reference proofs' failing build on it as the reproducing test;
     - `bench validate` ok.

     `config.py`'s missing clause-three check (G3) is a named gap for the join, not a blocker for the slice.
  3. The spike note records the exact TLC argv, the generated trace spec, the trace JSON shape, the interface (item 4) and the wall time per trace. A new jar, if any, is pinned in the note by URL and sha256 before `tool_versions` learns it.
  4. The DR-FM2 table is the D2 generator's third dimension (`formal-grader.md:290`), with one fixture per row. The two moved fixtures are updated in the design in the same commit as the implementation.
  5. The wave join lists `tasks/README.md`, G1's `prompt.md`, the design and the spike note, each with the commit that changed it (R-82 c5).
  6. No live run is required for this ruling.

## R-85 · 2026-09-30 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-PI-1..4: all four defaults granted with conditions; one name for `saturated`, the Codex reader reads `local_shell_call`, an unreadable blast radius makes every product-write indicator NA, PK-08 is a watch line not a ranked finding, and the trace shares the extractor's walk so tool counts have one home

- **Ruling:**
  - **DR-PI-1: report time, granted.** The section derives from `view`, board rows and the archived native records through the existing readers, stores nothing, and `bench report` is the normal path, so the indicators are emitted by default (IO) without a catalog bump. `context_growth.py` is the pattern (its docstring already reuses `grade/cost.py`'s `SESSION_TOTALS` / `ACP_MISSES_CALLS`, DM7). Promotion to a weight-0 grader is a catalog `0.6` change, and only after PI-T15's goldens hold on a second grid.
    - The new `tool_inputs` reader is a second walk over the record the extractor already walks (`claude_code.py:115` `tool_use`, `codex.py:41` `CALL_TYPES`, `copilot.py:79`). Two walks would give two counts of "tool calls per cell" (3.1's `tool_calls` rows and 4.3's `len(calls)`). One walker per harness yields the native tool blocks; the extractor projects `ToolCall`, the new reader projects `ToolInput`. PI-T6 asserts the trace length equals the extractor's `ToolCall` count on the same fixture record.
    - Codex's `CALL_TYPES` includes `local_shell_call` (`codex.py:41`). Section 4.3 lists only `function_call` and `custom_tool_call`, so Codex shell commands, where `git worktree add`, `audit-log.py` and `git config user.*` live, would be missed. The reader reads all three.
  - **DR-PI-2: versioned constants, granted.** Each constant carries a one-line comment naming the grid-1 measurement it sits against (F-1 medians 1.2–11.5; F-7 share 0.32 on / 0.01 off; F-2 deltas +18.4 k / +9.2 k). Comparisons use the unrounded `Decimal`; the 2-place rounding in section 4 is display only. A threshold change bumps `PACK_RULES_VERSION` and the PI-T15 golden in the same commit.
    - `saturated` has two definitions: section 4.5 (off arm passes every pair) and section 4.6 / DR-PI-2 (both arms ≥ 95%). Ruled: **`ceiling_off`** = `passes_off == n_pairs` (no gain was possible; used by 4.5's relabel, PK-05 and verdict rule 1's ceremony clause); **`saturated`** = both arms pass every pair (4.6's inconclusive reason); **`floor`** = both arms pass no pair. The 95% / 5% constants are dropped: below 20 pairs per arm they cannot differ from "every" / "none", so they are knobs with no measurement behind them.
  - **DR-PI-3: constant, granted.** The coverage test derives the expected prefixes from this repo's `docs/ai-forward-pack/INSTALL.md` deployment-map tables (first column) and `pack-doctor.py:733-734`'s surface lists, never from a hand-made fixture manifest: a fixture copy cannot drift, so a test over it cannot fail (CI6).
  - **DR-PI-4: no board export, granted.** `runs/` is git-ignored (`.gitignore:15`), so a JSON twin would not reach `/dream` either. A machine-readable emission is a recorded next step, added when a consumer exists.
  - **Other corrections found while reading:**
    1. **NA never becomes 0.** When `task.yaml` or `blast_radius` is unreadable, `product_write` is NA, so `test_first`, `stopped_without_product` and `diverted_delivery` are NA "blast radius not readable", not "zero product writes". Modeled share (4.2) is NA "no extra tokens" when `Σtokens_on − Σtokens_off ≤ 0`, not capped.
    2. **State order (section 2)** is evaluated one pack → not graded → no pairs → partial → full. In table order, an ungraded run reads "no pairs" (every cell is `not graded`).
    3. **One population for the pack effect.** Per-task Fisher, the Holm family and the pooled test exclude `stats.CONTAMINATION_PRONE` (`stats.py:26`) exactly as the board does, and the Method line prints the board's `exclusion_line`.
    4. **Token sum has one home.** `board.py:292` and `:490` each inline `sum(sum(b.values()) for b in c.tokens.values())`; S3 lifts it into one function and both board sites and the section call it.
    5. **`test_quality` leaves the TDD map.** R-68 item 3 retired it: its mechanical rung is `mutation_score`. The catalog entry at `metrics.yaml:59` is a separate finding for the join, not this slice.
    6. **PK-08 is not a ranked finding.** Its measure is not waste, and F-10 records that the clarify matcher recognised no question in grid-1. It renders as one watch line under Inconclusive with the means and n. "Where to improve" holds Verified waste and harm only.
    7. **Verdict rule 1** reads per intention: only the indicators in that intention's row can fire its miss.
- **Reasoning:**
  - Results first is served by keeping v1 report-only and small; every refusal above removes a second definition or a plausible wrong number rather than adding scope.
  - PI-T5's values are verified on this host: `(3,6,9,0)` = 0.0090, `(6,3,9,0)` = 0.2059, `(5,4,3,6)` = 0.6372, Holm first = 0.0543 (two-sided, `p(x) ≤ p(a)`). PI-T15's grid-1 expectations match the analysis (Codex 6.8×, +9.2 k; PK-01 / PK-02 cell sets from F-4 / F-5). The cell sets themselves are not re-observed here; PI-T15 observes them.
  - The Codex `local_shell_call` gap would have made PK-01 and PK-07 silently under-count on the one harness with the largest ratio, which is the class E15 names.
- **Conditions:**
  1. S2 lands the shared walker with PI-T6's count-equality assertion; the three `read()` functions keep their outputs byte-identical (the existing telemetry goldens prove it).
  2. Every NA above carries its reason string as a module constant, and PI-T4's class of test covers the four new NA paths (blast radius, no extra tokens, archive absent, unreadable record).
  3. The Method line prints `PACK_RULES_VERSION`, every threshold with its basis comment, the pair definition, the exclusion line, and names the gap that forces the Inferred share (no per-call record of fixed context).
  4. The design file is updated in the implementation commit for items 1–7 and the `ceiling_off` / `saturated` / `floor` names, so the design and the module agree (R-82 c5).
  5. Mutants: the listed seven, plus "Codex `local_shell_call` dropped" and "blast radius unreadable reads as zero product writes".
  6. No live run is required for this ruling; PI-T15 runs in the slow ring on the archived grids.

## R-86 · 2026-09-30 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-CAT-1: (C) with (A). `extraction_id` leaves `views.export` (provenance, like `grader_build`, is not a result); catalog `0.6` freezes with `scale: 4` because its `catalog_hash` moves; (B) refused; and the control's `board_exports[N].catalog == v` check is a defect that would block every catalog bump under an unchanged `EXPORT_VERSION`

- **Ruling:**
  - **(B) refused.** A re-pin of `0.5`'s goldens edits an entry present at the merge base: check (e) exists so that it cannot pass, and R-81 DR-R-10 already ruled "an audited exception is the control's own defect signature".
  - **(A) alone refused as a rule, granted as this instance.** `0.6` is legitimate only because `scale: 4` on `bug_claim_precision`, `model_conformance`, `model_non_vacuity` (`metrics.yaml:65,92,93`; R-84; `formal.py:546-552`'s flag) moves `catalog_hash`. A bump whose hash is unchanged is what R-81 refused ((a): "says content changed when it did not", ADR-0006:59). Two telemetry joins in one day show the recurrence rate; grid-3 will add more. The catalog version may not become the normaliser's version number.
  - **(C) granted: `extraction_id` is removed from `views.export` (`views.py:582`).** The docstring already promises "a pass's identity … is not part of it" (`:577-578`), `grader_build` (the grader's code hash) is not exported, and `extraction_id` (the normaliser's code hash, `normalize.py:85`) is its twin. Provenance stays where it lives: the `scores` rows and `RunView.Cell.extraction_id`, which `pack_improvement.py:887` and `context_growth.py:147` read from the view, not the bytes. No reader of the export bytes reads the field (grep of `views.export(`, `export_sha256`, `.export` goldens: `tests/test_views.py:481-1013`, `test_report.py:643,662`, `test_board.py:253`, `test_grade_runner.py:244-269,432`, `check_regrade.py`, `e2e/test_walking_skeleton.py:107`).
  - **What the control checks afterwards, and why US-4 is not weakened.** (a) compares result bytes: outcome, cause, validity, tokens, time split, every score and reason. A normaliser fix that moves one token, one `cost_usd` or one time split still fails (a), and old passes keep their extraction (`runner.py:224`, design `phase1-walking-skeleton.md:157`). A normaliser fix that moves nothing observable passes, which is what US-4 says (`harness-bench.md:277`: "if any fixture score changes"). The identity of the normaliser is versioned in the bytes it governs (`model_calls`, `tool_calls`, `scores`), not in the bytes the catalog governs — R-81's reasoning, one step further.
  - **Finding, ruled with it: the (d) pair check forbids a catalog bump.** `test_catalog_version.py:137` fails (d) unless `board_exports[N].catalog == v`; `board_exports['3'].catalog` is `'0.5'`, `EXPORT_VERSION` is `3` (`board.py:17`), and the board bytes carry `catalog_version` (`board.py` payload), so the `0.6` board export can never equal `tests/fixtures/board/3/`. A `0.6` freeze is impossible under the control as written unless `EXPORT_VERSION` bumps with no statistics change, which R-81 c2 forbids. Ruled: the pair `(v, N)` has one golden. A catalog freeze under an unchanged `N` records `board_export_version: N` in `versions[v]` (new entries only; `0.4`/`0.5` untouched, (e) holds) and its pair golden is `tests/fixtures/catalog/<v>/*.board.export` (already written by `freeze_catalog.py:109,115`). An `EXPORT_VERSION` bump under an existing catalog keeps `board_exports[N] = {catalog: v, golden}` and `tests/fixtures/board/<N>/`. (d) fails when neither names the current pair; (a)/(c) read whichever does.
- **Reasoning:**
  - The verified diff shows one moved field and it is a code hash. Failing US-4 on it is a false positive with a real cost: it forces a bump that ADR-0006 forbids, or a re-pin that (e) forbids. The only move that keeps both invariants is to take identity out of the result bytes (DM: derive don't store; R-78 DR-S-4 did the same for `leaderboard`).
  - The (d) defect was found by reading the control against the version it is about to receive, not by running it; it is the class R-81 named ("re-coupled the moment the first fix arrived"), now between catalog and export version.
- **Conditions:**
  1. **Red first on the 9.x root** (`test_catalog_version.py:196`): (i) a monkeypatched `normalize.extraction_id` leaves the control green and `"extraction_id" not in json.loads(views.export(...))["cells"][0]` (the `test_views.py:1013` shape); (ii) a catalog `9.2` frozen under the current `N` with `board_export_version` passes, and without it fails (d) with a message naming both maps.
  2. **EXP-A sweep in the same commit:** `bench/regrade-baseline-0.3.yaml` gains an append-only `runs_export_3` section measured by the Leader on the gate runs (`HB_GATE_RUNS`), proven as R-78 c4 was: old export minus `extraction_id`, re-canonicalised, byte-equal to the new; `tools/check_regrade.py` criterion 3 and `test_grade_runner.py:263` read it. `views.export`'s docstring names `extraction_id` beside `grading_id` as excluded.
  3. **Sequence, two commits as `0.5` (88fac26b → 61551a09):** commit 1 "release label 0.6": `bench/metrics.yaml` `version: "0.6"` with the history comment (`scale: 4` on the three formal metrics, R-84; `extraction_id` left the export, R-86), the three `scale: 4` entries, `formal.py:546-552`'s flag removed, the control change and condition 2's files. Commit 2 "freeze 0.6": `python tools/freeze_catalog.py` (the Windows form; `freeze_catalog.py:92` refuses an uncommitted `metrics.yaml`), then `tests/fixtures/catalog/0.6/{c44dd2b-no-heads,heads}.{export,board.export}` and the appended `versions['0.6']` entry (with `board_export_version: 3`); `uv run pytest -q tests/test_catalog_version.py tests/test_freeze_catalog.py tests/test_views.py tests/test_grade_runner.py`, full output in the join, not the exit code. `bench/catalog-freeze.yaml` stays Leader-owned.
  4. grid-2's re-grade and grid-3 grade under `0.6`; `formal-grader.md:352`'s "next catalog freeze" item closes citing R-86. A change after this freeze is `0.7` (R-59 c3's rule); a statistics change is `EXPORT_VERSION + 1` (R-81).
  5. **Class, not instance (CI):** `docs/lessons/defect-classes.md` gains "a code-identity field inside a result golden's bytes" (control: condition 1(i)) and "a freeze control whose pair check can only be satisfied by bumping the other version" (control: condition 1(ii)); sweep other goldens for `grader_build`, `grading_id` and `extraction_id` in their bytes and report the result in the join.
  6. No live run is required for this ruling; the Leader's `runs_export_3` measurement is on archived gate runs only.

## R-87 · 2026-10-03 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-1: (a) the Leader invokes the runner for every external dispatch, the Coordinator owns the dispatch's content; (b) and (c) refused; Claude Sonnet tracks are Agent-tool spawns with `model: sonnet` in a `coord worktree new` tree, spawned by the Coordinator where it has the Agent tool, else by the Leader under a recorded call budget; the runner's `claude` harness is unexercised and not a route until qualified

- **Ruling:**
  - **(a) granted.** For every Codex / Agy / Grok dispatch the Leader (`leader-e1e4`) invokes `prepare`, the qualification attestation, `run` and `status`, from a contract file and the compile-audit ids the Coordinator hands it. Verified: the runner admits a contract only when `AGENT_SESSION == contract["owner"]` (`coord-runner.py:330-331`, `RUN-OWNER`) and that owner is the live designated leader (`:708-710`, `RUN-LEADER`: "the runner never pins or steals leadership"); `coord leader who` at the time of this ruling: `leader-e1e4`, epoch 13, `live`, host `claude`, tree `primary`.
  - **(b) refused.** A lease hand-off per dispatch window changes the epoch, and the fence refuses every in-flight run on an epoch change (`:889-891`: "The leader epoch changed; no further dispatch is admitted"). It also blocks merges for the window. It trades a few short calls for a structural outage.
  - **(c) refused.** The kickoff (audit `al-01M418621NVAK4DJNDQ98XG5X6`) says "Development (coding) slices are federated across Codex / Agy / Grok" and "Dispatch Codex/Agy/Grok through `coord-runner.py`". Routing everything to Sonnet is a change of goal, which a decision request cannot author.
  - **How (a) fits "Leader merges and reports only".** That line is the kickoff's *Main-line budget* ("design and implementation run in workers"): a context budget on the Leader, not a list of commands it may type. The Leader's part of a dispatch is mechanical and bounded (condition 1). The dispatch *decision* (worker, harness, pinned model, deadline, fallback, compiled brief) stays with the Coordinator, whose kickoff duty includes "dispatches".
  - **Claude Sonnet worker tracks: Option 1.** The runner's `claude` harness exists in code (`coord-runner.py:373`, `:1052`) but has **0 of 106** runs in `.git/coord-runs` (the same grep pattern counts agy 66, codex 56, grok 102 rows). The plan's own rule (Order of operations, step 4) is "never dispatch onto an unexercised mode", so Option 2 is `unsupported` today. The Agent tool with `model: sonnet`, cwd set to the absolute path printed by `coord worktree new`, is the plan's measured "usable; the fallback for every track" mechanism (Harness capability table). Agent-tool worktree isolation stays off (refused twice, `coordination-phase1-finish-run.md`).
  - **Who spawns.** The Coordinator session, when it has the Agent tool: dispatch is its kickoff duty, and it keeps the Leader at merges. Verified in this harness: a sub-agent seat has the Agent tool (this Owner seat has it). *Inferred* for the Coordinator's session; if it has no Agent tool, the Leader spawns under condition 4.
- **Reasoning:** the lease holder is an exclusive resource (plan GO5(c)), so (a) is the only option that keeps one leader, keeps merges unblocked, and keeps the federation. The context cost is 3-4 calls per dispatch, which the Leader can measure and report. Option 1 for Sonnet is the only *exercised* mechanism; Option 2's evidence (served-model read-back, ownership check in the runner record) is stronger in principle, so it is not refused for ever: it is unqualified.
- **Conditions:**
  1. Per external dispatch the Leader's calls are `prepare`, the attestation, `run`, `status`, and `coord mail` at the join. The Leader never opens a brief, a transcript or a worker tree. The Coordinator's contract names the worker, the harness, the pinned model, `deadline_seconds`, the compile-audit ids and the fallback; the Leader does not edit it.
  2. The Leader's renewal loop stays live for the whole dispatch window (TTL cap 900 s; class COORD-C).
  3. Every Sonnet spawn pins `model: sonnet` (never a default), sets cwd to the `coord worktree new` path, exports `AGENT_SESSION=<track session>` in the worker's environment so the commit floor enforces, and never calls `EnterWorktree`. The served model id is read back from the first spawn's transcript at Q0 and recorded in the plan's harness table; if it is not `claude-sonnet-5`, Sonnet dispatch stops and the Coordinator raises a request.
  4. A spawn costs the spawner's context the brief's path or audit id and a report of at most 15 lines; a worker's transcript is never read into the Leader. If the Leader spawns, it records its calls against the Main-line budget in its audit entries (`--main-budget`).
  5. Q0 may add one `claude` worker to the qualification run (parallelism stays within the runner's 1-4). If it qualifies with the served id read back, Option 2 becomes a permitted route for Sonnet tracks, at the Coordinator's choice, and the plan's harness table records it.
- **What reverses it:** (a) → a delegated-dispatcher admission in the runner (a code change with its own ruling), never a lease hand-off. Option 1 → Option 2 for a track once condition 5 is met, or if condition 3's read-back cannot be done at Q0. The Coordinator-spawns default → Leader-spawns if the Coordinator's session is found to lack the Agent tool.

## R-88 · 2026-10-03 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-2: (c). X-A1 and X-D start as Claude Sonnet when their designs pass while B-1 is open; this ruling is the kickoff's "Owner review" for those two slices only; X-J1 and X-K1 stay Codex `gpt-6.1-sol` on codex-cli 0.160.0 after Q0; (b) refused; (a) refused

- **Ruling:**
  - **(c) granted, as the Owner review the kickoff requires** ("a stated fallback per slice (Claude Sonnet after an Owner review)"). The review covers X-A1 and X-D only.
  - **(a) refused.** X-A1 and X-D are on E1's critical path: X-C depends on X-B1, X-D, X-H1, X-A1; X-E on A, B1, D, F, G1, I (plan, Order of operations step 8; Tracks table). Holding them makes the operator's B-1 wall time the project's critical path.
  - **(b) refused.** The kickoff pins Codex at `gpt-6.1-sol` on codex-cli 0.160.0; the memory rule is never a default. `gpt-6-sol` on 0.156.0 is a different model and a different binary. (b) is also not executable: the approved wrapper pins `AGENT_SESSION=coord-opus-cq`, a retired seat, so the runner refuses with `RUN-OWNER` (`coord-runner.py:330`) before any model is served.
  - **A fact the Leader should know.** The kickoff says Codex is "verified served 2026-10-03", while the plan measured that no runner run has ever used 0.160.0 or `gpt-6.1-sol` (106 runs in `.git/coord-runs`). I did not locate the earlier verification; whatever it was, it was not the dispatch path. B-1 stands as the plan states it.
- **Reasoning:** the fallback clause exists for exactly this case: a harness that is not qualified when a slice is ready. R-4 applied the same move in phase 1 ("apply R-1's recorded fallback now"), with the condition that nobody widens a mode or upgrades a tool while the operator is offline. The same holds here: nobody edits the wrapper or its allow rule except the operator.
- **Conditions:**
  1. **Trigger:** (c) applies to a slice only when its design (W1-A, W1-D) passes its gate while B-1 is still open. If the operator clears B-1 and Q0 qualifies Codex 0.160.0 / `gpt-6.1-sol` (served id read back) before that moment, the slice goes to Codex as planned. A slice already started on Sonnet is not moved mid-track: `engine.py` and `config.py` have one owner per phase (hub-file table).
  2. The fallback runs under R-87 Option 1: `model: sonnet` pinned, own `coord worktree new` tree, the same compiled brief, the same red-first exit evidence. The Coordinator updates the Tracks rows for X-A1 and X-D to "Claude Code · Sonnet (R-88; B-1 open)" and records the measured evidence, as R-4's first condition did.
  3. X-J1 and X-K1 stay Codex, conditioned on Q0: the operator-approved wrapper at a Leader-owned stable path, pinning the 0.160.0 exe, `{"model":"gpt-6.1-sol","model_reasoning_effort":"high"}`, `AGENT_SESSION=leader-e1e4` and the invoking tree (B-1's operator action), then one smoke turn with the served model read back. If B-1 is still open when W1-J's TLC passes, the Coordinator raises a new request; this ruling does not pre-authorise a Sonnet fallback for J1 or K1.
  4. `.tools/harness` is not changed by anyone: it is the cells' pinned build and part of the engine identity (plan B-1).
  5. The E1 report to the operator states that the engine-core E1 slices ran on Sonnet, not Codex, and names B-1 as the reason, so the routing intent's shortfall is visible, not silent.
- **What reverses it:** B-1 cleared and Q0 passed before W1-A or W1-D passes its gate → Codex, no fallback. A Codex failure at Q0 after B-1 is cleared → a new request for J1/K1; not this ruling.

## R-89 · 2026-10-03 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-3: (a), resolved now from grid-4's facts: the E1 pilot ring and mini grid run on `cc-opus` (claude-code, `claude-opus-5-5`) with k = 3; the operator confirms or overrides at the demo (B-3); no other grid

- **Measured** (2026-10-03, `runs/grid-4`, the engine facts `cell.launch_intent` → `cell.outcome` in `events/engine-1790973057.jsonl`, 276 cells planned, 276 outcomes; `matrix.yaml`: `bench-matrix/1`, 23 tasks A1..G2, `repetitions: 2`, packs on/off, three combos):

  | combo | harness · model | n | mean wall / cell | median | max | outcomes |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | `cc-opus` | claude-code · `claude-opus-5-5` | 92 | **176.6 s** | 92.1 s | 1812.6 s | 91 completed, 1 timed_out |
  | `copilot-sol` | copilot · `gpt-6-sol` | 92 | 195.2 s | 89.9 s | 1815.3 s | 91 completed, 1 timed_out |
  | `codex-sol` | codex · `gpt-6-sol` | 92 | 231.6 s | 140.4 s | 1806.0 s | 90 completed, 2 timed_out |

  Wall is launch-intent to outcome, so it includes the workspace build and the handshake. The mean turn (`turn_ms`) orders the same way (173.1 / 189.4 / 226.6 s).
- **Ruling:**
  - **(a), with the combo named here rather than "read at demo time":** `cc-opus`, k = 3. `runs/` is untracked (artifact classes: ignored), so a number read only at the demo would leave no record; this ruling is the record. The E1 phasing row (architecture `:315`) defines the shape: a 2-arm pilot ring (`off`, `candidate`) on one combo, then a 2-arm mini comparison of 1 task × 1 combo × k reps, expected `inconclusive (underpowered)`.
  - **(b) stays open to the operator** at the demo (B-3): a named combo and k override this ruling without a new request. Any combo in the matrix is eligible as a benchmark *subject*, `copilot-sol` included: the kickoff's "never a worker" rule is about coding workers, and grid-4 itself ran Copilot cells.
- **Reasoning:** the cheapest real spend that exercises the whole path is the lowest measured mean wall; the measurement exists and says `cc-opus`. k = 3 gives three recorded pairs, enough for the verdict rule to reach `inconclusive (underpowered)` rather than `inconclusive (not recorded)` (ADR-0020 §2's order), while 2 arms × 3 reps × 1 task is six cells.
- **Conditions:**
  1. **Pre-registration:** the demo registers a minimum recorded pairs per verdict of 3 or fewer (`enterprise-evaluation.md:329`), and says so. With k = 3 the mini grid yields exactly 3 pairs (paired by task, harness, repetition); a minimum above 3 would turn the expected verdict into `inconclusive (not recorded)`.
  2. **The number is a proxy.** grid-4's tasks are A1..G2, not S1. S1's wall per cell is *Inferred* until the pilot ring measures it; the power input names grid-4 as the source run (ADR-0020 §1: "hours from the named source runs' measured mean wall per cell") and the pilot's own measurement replaces it for the final power step.
  3. **Spend, Inferred from the proxy:** the pilot ring (2 arms × 1 task; *assume:* one rep per arm satisfies ADR-0016 §7's pilot coverage of each (task, combo, harness); confirm when W1-C fixes the pilot shape; if false, the pilot's cell count is whatever the registration refuses without) plus six mini-grid cells, about 8 cells × 177 s ≈ 24 min of model wall. claude-code cells authenticate with `HB_CLAUDE_OAUTH_TOKEN` (`profiles.py:35`), the same subscription as the Leader and the Sonnet workers; the operator decides at the demo whether to run the cells while Sonnet workers are mid-dispatch.
  4. **No other grid.** The kickoff allows only "E1's own pilot and mini grid". One re-pilot after a pilot-gate defect is part of the pilot (architecture `:72`'s loop) and is reported to the operator with its cell count; a second re-pilot, a different task, or any ring beyond these two is a new request.
  5. The combo and k are written into the E1 demo's `bench-matrix/2` pilot ring file (`bench/rings/pilot.yaml`, X-A1's owned path) with `model: claude-opus-5-5` pinned explicitly, never by default.
- **What reverses it:** the operator naming a different combo or k at the demo; a pilot-ring measurement of S1 on `cc-opus` that exceeds the slot budget (900 s per cell in grid-4's plan) → the Coordinator re-costs and the operator chooses; grid-4's data being absent at the demo does not reverse it, because the measurement is recorded here.

### Ruling 90 — 2026-10-03 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-4: (a). One property grader records the eleven property metrics, narrowed by the task's property through one catalog property: tag; it runs the hidden tests through correctness.grade(); tasks keep correctness so pass_at_1 stays; (b) deferred, (c) refused; the count is eleven (ADR-0019), the plan's ten is a miscount

- **Ruling:**
  - **(a) granted, and not only as the cheaper option: it is the only option that can grade every property.** The spec's primary is "1 when the stated ask's hidden tests pass and the hidden check passes" (`enterprise-evaluation.md:178`; security `:244`; rework `:263`; no-guessing `:269`; simplicity `:278`). The rework row needs "turn-1 hidden tests on the turn-1 snapshot" and "turn-2 hidden tests on the final tree". The correctness grader grades the final tree only (`correctness.grade_cell`, `grade(inp.archive / "ws", …)`, `correctness.py:513-519`), so under (c) the property grader would still have to run tests itself for rework, and (c) would then be two mechanisms for one quantity. Under (a) one grader owns every test run that a property needs, on whichever tree the property names.
  - **(c) refused.** `runner.py:12-23` (Verified): each cell is graded by its graders "in catalog order", each through its own `grade_cell` with its own `out_dir` and `work_root` (`:328-333`), and GradedOncePerPass checks only the written key set. Nothing passes one grader's output to another; adding that is a new ordering coupling with no invariant behind it, which is what W0 section 7 says.
  - **(b) refused for E1, open as a later decision.** Dropping `correctness` removes `pass_at_1`, the board's primary (`board.py:190,194`), from ten tasks for a test-run saving that is measured, not known. ADR-0019 item 4 makes a missing `pass_at_1` *not recorded*, so (b) would not corrupt the board, but it is a leaderboard-scope change that nobody asked for. If the measured double run is material, (b) returns as a request with the number.
  - **The count: eleven, the ADR wins.** ADR-0019 item 2 names `property_check_pass` plus ten secondaries (Verified). The plan's "ten" in the hub-file row for `bench/metrics.yaml` (`coordination-eval-campaign.md:134`), W1-G's exit evidence (`:183`) and X-G1's (`:209`) conflates the ten *tasks* (`:30`, correct) with the metrics. This is a miscount, not a decision: the Coordinator corrects the three rows to eleven at its next plan edit; no track widens.
- **Reasoning:** the dispatch rule in `design-phase3-graders` ("the applicable set is every `kind: score` catalog metric of those graders") was written when every grader's metrics applied to every task that named it. A property grader is the first whose metrics are partitioned by the task's property, so the rule gains one clause rather than an exception: a metric with a `property:` tag is applicable only when it equals the task's `property.name` (or has no tag, as `property_check_pass`). The runner already hands each grader its applicable set (`inp.metrics`, `runner.py:329`), so the grader never computes its own list: one definition (DM7).
- **Conditions:**
  1. **One narrowing, three readers.** The `property:` tag lives in the catalog entry (W1-G); `runner.applicable` applies it; `readiness.py`'s "a metric a task's graders name and `expected` omits fails readiness" (W0 section 2) reads the *narrowed* set through the same function, so a security task declares expected values for `property_check_pass` and `exploit_probes_blocked` only. A metric outside the narrowed set has no row (it is not "NA not built").
  2. **One definition of "hidden tests pass".** The property grader calls `correctness.grade(ws, task_dir, oracle, out_dir, run_dir, timeout, work_dir)` (`correctness.py:196`) with the pass's `grading_step_timeout`, in its own grading copy (ADR-0018 §1), never a re-implementation and never the correctness grader's files. For rework it calls it once per graded tree (turn-1 snapshot, final).
  3. **The double run is measured and its disagreement is a finding.** The property evidence records `hidden_tests_ms` per tree, and, on the final tree, whether its inner result agrees with the pass's own `pass_at_1` row for the same cell (read from the pass after both are written, by X-E's readiness/discrimination path, not by the grader). A disagreement is a pilot-gate item named "hidden tests non-deterministic", never silently resolved either way (IO1; EV-14).
  4. **Strategy helpers, not graders.** `rework.py`, `noguess.py`, `diffstats.py` (W0 section 9) are called by `property.grade_cell` and are not registered in `GRADERS`; a second registered grader for a property is the (c) coupling by another name and is refused.
  5. **The dispatch-rule amendment is written down**: `design-phase3-graders` gains the `property:` clause as an amendment citing R-90 (W1-G's design carries the text; the Documentation Steward's follow-up lands it), with a red test: a security task graded by `property` yields rows for exactly `property_check_pass` and `exploit_probes_blocked`, and GradedOncePerPass is green.
  6. Scenario-7 `pass_at_1` (ADR-0019 item 3) is untouched by this ruling; it stays the formal grader's.
- **What reverses it:** a measured grading-span cost of the second test run that moves the pilot's grading envelope materially → (b) by a new request with the number; a property whose "hidden tests" cannot be expressed through `correctness.grade()`'s oracle runners (`unittest`, `dotnet`, `pytest`, `correctness.py:202`) → a seam request to X-F, not a third test runner. The eleven count reverses only by an ADR-0019 amendment.

## R-91 · 2026-10-03 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-5: (a). The Sonnet seat for Agent-tool workers is the alias `sonnet`, served id `claude-sonnet-5-5`, recorded per track; the pin moves from an unverified id to a verified one and R-87 condition 3 is re-stated against it; the four W0 reviews count; the deviation from the kickoff's `claude-sonnet-5` goes to the operator in the next scheduled report; (b) stays available through Q0, (c) and (d) refused

- **Facts:** (Leader, 2026-10-03, reported to this seat; the RV-TA transcript is not in the audit log or `.agents`, so it is *reported*, not located by me) the first Agent-tool spawn under `model: sonnet` reported `claude-sonnet-5-5` and recorded the mismatch itself; `claude -p --model claude-sonnet-5` on Claude Code 2.1.288 returned a `modelUsage` key `claude-sonnet-5`. **Verified by me:** the Agent tool's `model` parameter is the enum `sonnet | opus | haiku | fable` (this session's own tool schema), so no full id can be passed; the plan's harness table already said the alias's served id was "**not verified**: read it back" (`coordination-eval-campaign.md:78`); the runner's `claude` harness has 0 of 106 runs (R-87); the operator's memory rule says "Claude subagents: always pass `model`" and "verify the served model at qualification" — it asks for an explicit pin and a read-back, not for one id (`stipulate-model.md`).
- **Ruling:**
  - **(a) granted.** For Agent-tool workers the pin is `model: sonnet`, and the recorded served id is `claude-sonnet-5-5`. The kickoff's `claude-sonnet-5` was written before any read-back (plan `:78`); the read-back is the measurement the kickoff asked for, and it says the alias serves 5.5 on this account. **R-87 condition 3 is re-stated:** Sonnet dispatch stops only when a spawn's served id differs from `claude-sonnet-5-5`; the hold the Leader placed was the control firing as written, and it lifts with this ruling.
  - **Why this is not R-88 (b) in reverse.** R-88 refused `gpt-6-sol` because an older model on an older binary through a wrapper that would be refused anyway is not the kickoff's intent and the pinned model *is* reachable with operator action (B-1). Here the pinned id is unreachable through the only exercised mechanism, the served model is the same family one step newer, and worker output enters no measurement: benchmark cells pin their own model in the profiles (US-11), so the worker seat's id is a labour fact, not a data fact.
  - **(b) refused as the route now, kept as Q0's option.** Qualifying the `claude` harness is one smoke worker in Q0's run (R-87 condition 5 already permits it). Until it is qualified, routing Sonnet through it is dispatch onto an unexercised mode. After it is qualified, the Coordinator may use it for a coding slice when it wants a `--model claude-sonnet-5` pin or the runner's evidence; that is a per-slice choice recorded in the Tracks row, not a split seat.
  - **(c) refused.** One named seat served by two models by rule makes the Harness · Model column mean two things for "Sonnet". A per-slice runner choice after qualification (above) is allowed; a standing split is not.
  - **(d) refused.** The question is resolvable here (NG: ask only when consequential *and* unresolvable). It is consequential enough to be *reported*: the next scheduled operator report (E1 demo ready, a phase converging, or a block) carries one line, "Sonnet workers served `claude-sonnet-5-5` via the alias; the kickoff wrote `claude-sonnet-5`; R-91", and the operator may reverse it then.
  - **The four W0 reviews on 5.5 (RV-SEC, RV-DS, RV-PAT, RV-SIM) count, and RV-TA counts.** They ran under an explicit pin (`model: sonnet`) with the served id recorded or recordable; nothing in their output depends on which Sonnet. Condition 2 applies to each.
- **Conditions:**
  1. Every Sonnet spawn passes `model: sonnet` explicitly (never omitted) and its report's first line records the served id it reads from its own context. A spawn whose report lacks the id is read back from its transcript by the Coordinator, not re-run.
  2. The Tracks table's model cell for Sonnet rows reads "Sonnet (`model: sonnet`, served `claude-sonnet-5-5`, R-91)"; the harness table's "not verified" cell is replaced with the measured id and the date.
  3. The audit entries of the Leader's and Coordinator's dispatches carry the served id, so `/session-profiler` can count work per served model.
  4. The read-back control stays live: a spawn served anything other than `claude-sonnet-5-5` stops Sonnet dispatch and raises a request, as R-87 condition 3 intended. A newer alias resolution is a *new* ruling, not a silent update.
  5. The operator's reversal path is cheap and stated: if the operator wants `claude-sonnet-5` exactly, the route is (b) after Q0 qualifies it, and the Coordinator re-plans the Sonnet rows; nothing already delivered is re-run.
- **What reverses it:** the operator naming `claude-sonnet-5` at the next report; the alias resolving to a third id (condition 4); a measured defect in a 5.5-authored artifact that the operator attributes to the model (none expected; recorded here so the claim is falsifiable).

### Ruling 92 — 2026-10-03 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-6 (the request says DR-5; DR-5 is R-91): (d) bounded. Grok served grok-4.6-build under -m grok-4.7 at Q0 while w4-lr served grok-4.7-build on the same argv and grok models still lists 4.7 as default; one re-qualification run by the Leader now, served model read per response; unsupported at W1-G or W1-B's gate sends X-G1, X-B1, X-H1 and ENV-A to Sonnet (this is their Owner review); (a), (b)-now and (c) refused

- **Recorded as DR-6.** The request `req-01M41BS9Y7D1ANHSNDYM9N2H08` is labelled "DR-5" in its text; DR-5 is the Sonnet pin mismatch (R-91). This ruling answers the Grok question under the id DR-6.
- **Facts, Verified by me on 2026-10-03:**
  - The Grok argv is byte-identical in `.git/coord-runs/q0-e1e4/contract.json` and `.git/coord-runs/w4-lr/contract.json`: `grok agent --no-leader -m grok-4.7 --reasoning-effort high stdio`. The CLI is 1.0.41 in both (Q0 README `:66`; `qualify-5`).
  - Served model, read per response from `~/.grok/sessions/<tree>/<session>/chat_history.jsonl` `model_id`: **w4-lr (2026-09-28): `grok-4.7-build` × 61. Q0 (2026-10-03): `grok-4.6-build` × 8**; `summary.json` `current_model_id: grok-4.6`. The Q0 session never contains the string `grok-4.7`. Every other session from 2026-09-27 to 09-28 (eleven, w3-ciopt3 through w4-lr) has `current_model_id: grok-4.7`.
  - `env -u XAI_API_KEY grok models`, run now: "You are logged in with grok.com. Default model: grok-4.7. Available: grok-4.7 (default), grok-4.7-build-fast, grok-4.6, grok-4.5". The subscription serves 4.7 today, so the cause is not an entitlement loss, not the argv, not the CLI version. It is a service-side routing of one session, and the records cannot say whether it was transient.
  - `coord-runner.py` has a served-model check for Copilot only (`copilot_model_evidence`, `:175`); for Grok it checks the transport (`:375`) and nothing about the model. Q0's read-back was manual. **The pin control for Grok was a memoir until Q0 read the session store.**
- **Ruling:**
  - **(d) granted, bounded, and the operator check is already done.** The re-qualification is one Grok smoke worker through the runner (the Q0 shape: same argv, deadline 600 s, `XAI_API_KEY` removed), started by the Leader (R-87) as soon as the lease loop allows; no operator action precedes it, because the only check the operator could do (`grok models`) is above. Grok is **qualified** only if every response `model_id` in that session starts with `grok-4.7`; a single `grok-4.6*` response leaves it `unsupported`.
  - **Deadline and fallback.** If Grok is still unsupported when W1-G or W1-B passes its gate, the track that depends on it (X-G1 on W1-G, the E1 critical path; X-B1 on W1-B) goes to Claude Sonnet under R-87 Option 1 and R-91's pin; so do X-H1 and the ENV-A fix when their turn comes while Grok is unsupported. **This ruling is the kickoff's Owner review for X-G1, X-B1, X-H1 and ENV-A.** X-G3 is E3: if Grok is still unsupported then, a new request, not this ruling. A slice started on Sonnet is not moved back.
  - **(a) refused as stated.** It holds four tracks on the operator for a cause the records cannot show; the records show a service-side drift, and the measurement that resolves it is a 600-second run, not a person.
  - **(b) now refused.** No Grok track starts before W1-G or W1-B passes (Q0 README `:70`); rerouting on one sample forfeits the federation for nothing. Agy as the target is refused as a default: the plan caps external harnesses at 2 concurrent and Agy already carries X-B2, X-C, X-H2 in E1; the Coordinator may place one Grok slice on a free Agy slot by a plan edit, never by this ruling.
  - **(c) refused.** `grok-4.6-build` is a different model from the pin, and, worse, the pin did not move: the *served* model drifted under an unchanged `-m`. That is the US-13 class ("defaults drift and differ per account", `stipulate-model.md`) seen from the other side, and accepting it would turn the read-back into a formality. R-91 accepted a drift because the alias was the only selector and the served id was recorded; here the selector is explicit and was not honoured.
- **Conditions:**
  1. **The served model is read from responses, never from argv or a model list.** The qualification record and every Grok join read `chat_history.jsonl` `model_id` (count per id) and `summary.json` `current_model_id` for the dispatch's session, and write them in the Tracks row and the Q0 README (a "Q0b" section, append-only). This read is scripted before the second Grok dispatch (a stdlib reader over the session directory, Coordinator-owned under `tools/`), so it is a control, not a habit (CI6).
  2. **A later drift is a finding, not a silent accept.** A Grok dispatch whose responses are not all `grok-4.7*` is recorded as served `<id>` in the Tracks row; its commit is judged on its gate evidence (tests, red SHA), because worker output enters no measurement (R-91's reasoning); Grok dispatch pauses and the Coordinator raises a request.
  3. **Class, not instance (CI).** `docs/lessons/defect-classes.md` gains "pin passed, served model unchecked" with three instances (US-13 Copilot default, R-91 Sonnet alias, R-92 Grok routing) and the control of condition 1. The runner's missing Grok/Codex/Agy served-model check is a finding for the pack (`docs/ai-forward-pack/` is managed; not authored here), reported in the next operator report.
  4. The phase-1 Grok rules stand: `XAI_API_KEY` removed on every call; subscription login only.
  5. **Operator line for the next report:** "Grok served `grok-4.6-build` under `-m grok-4.7` at Q0 (2026-10-03); w4-lr served `grok-4.7-build` with the same argv and CLI on 09-28; `grok models` still lists grok-4.7 as the default. Nothing to do now. If the re-qualification also serves 4.6, please check grok.com's model routing for this subscription, and say whether grok-4.6 is acceptable as the Grok pin (R-92 (c) is refused until you say so)."
- **What reverses it:** the re-qualification serving `grok-4.7*` on every response → the Grok tracks run as planned; serving `grok-4.6*` again → the fallback above per trigger; the operator naming `grok-4.6` acceptable → (c) by the operator's decision, recorded as a ruling with the served id, as R-91 did for Sonnet.

- request: req-01M41BS9Y7D1ANHSNDYM9N2H08 · ruled by: owner-fable · at: 2026-10-03T17:13:51Z

### Ruling 93 — 2026-10-03 · Owner seat (Fable) · DR-7: grid runs surface the hidden-test disagreement count ((b) in substance), as a warning line with cell ids in report section 3 beside ADR-0020's exclusions, not in the EV-20 header (a spec-fixed identity and eligibility list); it changes no verdict, no exclusion and no eligibility; the pilot gate keeps its item; EVU-4 holds

- **Ruling:**
  - **Grid runs surface the count: (b) in substance, granted.** R-90 condition 3 says a disagreement is "never silently resolved either way (IO1; EV-14)"; a grid run that shows nothing resolves it silently for exactly the runs that produce verdicts. RV-DS 10 (`docs/design/reviews/eval-review-ds.md:37`) is right on the symptom: a flaky hidden test moves `property_check_pass` against `pass_at_1` with no flag. The spec's own pattern is the Reliability NFR (`enterprise-evaluation.md:401`): defect classes the pilot gate checks are "Counted per grid, the target is 0" and "reported, not hidden". A disagreeing cell is not *lost*, so the NFR does not literally require this line; IO1 and R-90 do.
  - **The home is section 3, not the EV-20 header.** EV-20's header is a fixed list — "the question, the arms with pack revisions, the pre-registration hash, the engine baseline, the recorded defect fixes, the MDE per property and the power-analysis id" (`:392`) — and every field is campaign identity or eligibility. A per-grid anomaly count is neither; adding it widens the spec's header, and it would be the one line a reader could mistake for an eligibility fact. ADR-0020 §2 already gives section 3 a listing of "exclusions (calibration, invalid, lost cells) … with id and reason"; the disagreement line sits beside that listing as a **warning that changes nothing**, the class the harness-bench report already has (`report/html.py:415`, `validity-warnings`: "flags that do not change validity", R-24/R-26 c5, R-28). Section 3 is X-H2's own file (`report/campaign_section.py`), so no hub file gains an owner.
  - **Not an exclusion, not an eligibility fact.** The verdict reads `property_check_pass`; the disagreement is evidence that the task's hidden tests are non-deterministic on that cell, which affects both arms alike. The cell stays in its pair. Making it an exclusion or an ineligibility reason would be a new pre-registration rule (`:329` "exclusion rules"), which only P1 sets; if the operator wants it, that is a registration decision, not a report line.
  - **The pilot gate keeps its item** (`GateItem(kind="hidden-tests-nondeterministic")`, W0 §7 condition 3). The pilot stops a registration; the grid line reports what the pilot could not see at k = 1.
- **Reasoning:** one function (`readiness.hidden_test_disagreements`, X-E, built anyway), one consumer per surface (the pilot gate, section 3); no store, no grader change, no second definition. The grader-side `hidden_tests_agree` half of RV-DS 10 stays rejected: it is the (c) coupling R-90 refused.
- **Conditions:**
  1. Section 3 renders, for a campaign run, one line beside the exclusions: `hidden tests disagreed on <n> cells: <cell ids>` (ids listed, as ADR-0020 §2 lists exclusions), and renders nothing when n = 0 — so **EVU-4 holds** (`:809`: no campaign, no verdict section, harness-bench golden unchanged). It is read from `readiness.hidden_test_disagreements(run_dir, grading_id)` after the pass completes; section 3 never reads grading evidence itself.
  2. **Red first (X-H2):** a campaign-report fixture with one seeded disagreement (the final-tree `hidden_tests_pass` set opposite to the cell's `pass_at_1` row) shows the line with that cell id; the same fixture without the seed shows no such element. The DOM check joins the EVU list as the next free id; W1-H names it.
  3. The line's wording says what it is: a warning, not a verdict input ("verdicts unchanged; the task's hidden tests were non-deterministic on these cells"). The UX lens reviews the copy with the rest of section 3.
  4. The campaign record is untouched by this ruling: the count is derived at report time from the pass (DM: derive, don't store).
- **What reverses it:** P1 adding a disagreement rule to the pre-registration's exclusion rules (then the cell is an exclusion with that reason, listed where exclusions are, and this warning line is redundant for registered runs); a spec amendment to EV-20's header list (then the header, by the spec, not by this ruling).

- request: req-01M41CV2MNPQENPMEMP01QF3ZY · ruled by: owner-fable · at: 2026-10-03T17:30:43Z

### Ruling 94 — DR-8 (W1-D, unnumbered in the request): (A). ADR-0017 s1 is amended: telemetry/* is run side (the engine reads every native record at cell end for cause and SPEND, engine.py:661,854; profiles.py:262) and gateway = file_hash(bench/gateway.yaml) is a grade-side component; the measured cost under the old list is 6 run-to-grade edges in driver, engine, profiles, not 5; (B) and (C) refused; W1-D writes the Amendment 1 note on design/eval-identity in ADR-0006's form

- **Recorded as DR-8.** The request carries no DR id; DR-7 is R-93. The request's evidence line says "5 more allowlist entries"; the author corrected it to 6 edges in 3 modules before this ruling, and the corrected number is the one verified below.
- **Facts, Verified by me on 2026-10-03 (main `c812195b`, the branch point):**
  - Every telemetry file executes on the cell path, not only by import. At every cell end `engine.py:661` calls `_read_records(launcher, home, session_id)`, which is `[launcher.read(path) for path in launcher.records(...)]` (`:854`); `ProfileLauncher.read` is `READERS[self.harness](path)` (`profiles.py:261-262`), the three readers bound at `profiles.py:212`. The extractions feed `_classify` (`:662,:786`: `normalize.classify(errors)` at `:787`, the cell's recorded cause) and `_spend` (`:668,:857-867`: `normalize.totals`, `record_unreadable`, the `SPEND` row that drives the spend-cap stop). `driver.py:237,246,290` classify an adapter exception through `normalize.is_auth_failure` and `normalize.classify`. So a change to any of `telemetry/__init__.py`, `normalize.py`, `claude_code.py`, `codex.py`, `copilot.py` can change what a measured cell records while it executes: ADR-0017 s1's own test for run side.
  - The edge count is **6 run-to-grade edges in 3 modules** under the ADR's list, counted as (importing file -> imported telemetry file): `driver.py:39` -> `telemetry/__init__` (`ProviderError`) and -> `telemetry/normalize`; `engine.py:46` -> `telemetry/normalize`; `profiles.py:24` -> `telemetry/claude_code`, -> `telemetry/codex`, -> `telemetry/copilot`. The request's "5" was wrong; the correction stands.
  - `bench/gateway.yaml` is read by `grade/judge.py:94`, `report/judges.py:256` and `gateway/calibration.py:184,262` (all `config.load_gateway`), and at validate time by `config.py:381-382`. No run-class module reads it on a cell's path. It fixes the judge model and invocation that produce every judged score, and no existing manifest component covers it (`catalog` hashes `metrics.yaml` + `rubrics/`; `prices` hashes `prices.yaml`).
  - Grade-side readers of telemetry (`grade/cost.py`, `grade/runner.py`, `gateway/pipeline.py:169`, `views.py`, `report/pack_improvement.py`) are grade -> run edges, which the direction test allows.
- **Ruling:**
  - **(A) granted.** ADR-0017 s1 is amended: `telemetry/*` (all five files) is **run** side; a new grade-side component `gateway` = `file_hash(bench/gateway.yaml)`, `""` when the file is absent (`config.py:381`: optional until the Leader's measured turn, R-70). The design's rule in section 4.1 ("a module used by both is run; run dominates") is the ADR's rule made testable, and it is adopted as the reading of s1.
  - **(B) refused.** Six allowed run -> grade edges would be an allowlist whose every entry says "this grade-class file runs inside a cell": a class table that is wrong, with a side table that says so. Under (B) a fix to `normalize.classify` or to a reader's usage parse would be admitted with `scope: grade` and re-graded, while the cells it affected keep the cause and `SPEND` the old code recorded; the verdict would then read a grade-side identity that matches and a run-side record that does not. That is the eligibility rule (s5) reporting a false match, the one failure the freeze exists to prevent.
  - **(C) refused.** The design's section 4.3 already refuses a third class on its own evidence; one mis-listed glob is not a reason for one.
- **Reasoning:** the s1 list was written from what the modules are *for* (telemetry is extraction, extraction is grading) rather than from where they *execute*; the engine's cell-end read (`engine.py:661`, "read once: the provider-error scan and the spend") makes them part of the measurement. The cost of the correct class is real and accepted: a telemetry fix after a baseline is `scope: run`, so cells launched before it are re-run (operator decision DI6, ADR-0017 s6: "re-run them"). The cost of the wrong class is a verdict shown on a stale record.
- **An ADR amendment note is needed, and W1-D writes it.** ADR-0017 is accepted and its s1 list is the text the classification table in `identity.py` must match; a table that silently disagrees with its ADR is the "load-bearing, needs review at the gate" follow-up failing on its first review. ADR-0017 is not a hub file in the plan, and the request's blast radius names its s1 text as W1-D's. So W1-D (the author of this request) appends the note to `docs/adr/0017-engine-identity-and-freeze.md` on `design/eval-identity`, and it lands with W1-D's gate merge (the precedent is R-90 c5: the design carries the text). The W0 s9 note goes through the Coordinator's answer to W1-D's seam request `req-01M41DJDH521RE0M4QJJK1FRTD`, not through this ruling.
- **Conditions:**
  1. **The amendment's form** follows ADR-0006: a header bullet "**Amended (2026-10-03, ruling R-94; design `eval-identity` section 4.2):** `telemetry/*` moves to the run side; `gateway` (`bench/gateway.yaml`) is a grade-side component. See 'Amendment 1' below", and an "Amendment 1" section that (i) restates the s1 list with `telemetry/*` under run and `gateway` under grade, (ii) gives the reason in one sentence with the cell-end read cited (`engine.py:661,854`; `profiles.py:262`), (iii) records the accepted cost (a telemetry fix is `scope: run`; DI6), and (iv) records this as the first review of the "classification is load-bearing" follow-up. The s1 body text is not rewritten; the amendment is appended (the ADR's history is the record).
  2. **The table is the single definition; tests pin the two decisions.** X-D's `identity.py` lists every `telemetry/*` file as `run` and the `gateway` component as `grade`; `tests/test_identity.py::test_side_partitions_every_component` (design section 12) covers the partition, and X-D adds one red-first test per decision: an edit to `telemetry/normalize.py` changes the run-side hash and not the grade-side hash; a `bench/gateway.yaml` edit (and its absence -> presence) changes the grade-side hash and not the run-side hash.
  3. **The direction test's allowlist stays at three.** `RUN_IMPORTS_GRADE_ALLOWED` holds only the `config.py` validate-time pairs the design names (`config.py:410,466,467`); no telemetry entry; a fourth entry of any kind is a decision request, not an allowlist edit.
  4. **`gateway` absent -> present is a grade-side fix, by design.** When `bench/gateway.yaml` lands after a baseline, the effective grade identity changes and every campaign run's verdicts are withheld until a new grading pass (s6). That is correct: judged metrics move from "no qualified judge" to scored, and the verdict must say under which fix. The baseline command does not require the file to exist (s3 is unchanged).
  5. **The cost is measured, not modelled (IO).** `defect_fix.admitted` rows with `scope: run` whose `changes` keys all lie under `src/harness_bench/telemetry/` are the measured price of this class. The design's section 4.2 "provisional" is removed and cites R-94; the request record keeps "5" as written and this ruling carries the 6.
- **What reverses it:** the engine ceasing to read native records at cell end (for example, spend and cause taken from the ACP turn only, so `_read_records` goes): the readers would then execute only in grading, and a new request with the import graph may move `claude_code`, `codex`, `copilot` to grade while `normalize` and `telemetry/__init__` stay run. Two telemetry-only run-scope fixes in one campaign (condition 5's rows) that each forced a re-run for a change that touched only tool-call or trace extraction: then the cure is splitting usage-and-error extraction (run) from tool-and-trace extraction (grade) into separate files, by request with the rows, never a per-function class.
