### Ruling 95 — DR-9 (W1-G also_graded_by, unnumbered in the request): (A). R-90 conditions 1 and 6 amended: the one function applicable(catalog, graders, prop) carries two clauses, the property narrowing and owner = first of [grader, *also_graded_by] the task names, so each (cell, metric) has one row; scenario-7 pass_at_1 stays the formal grader's through also_graded_by: [formal] on the entry, whose bytes change in 0.7; (B) and (C) refused; the key is validated at once; formal emits pass_at_1 only when it owns it, proven through a real pass

- **Recorded as DR-9** (the request carries no DR id; DR-8 is R-94). Ruled from the worktree `owner/ruling-r95` at main `1ceea651`.
- **Facts, Verified by me on 2026-10-03:**
  - `applicable(catalog, graders)` (`grade/runner.py:161-168`) keys every `kind: score` metric by its one `grader` string; `pass_at_1` is `grader: correctness` (`bench/metrics.yaml:45`); a second entry with the same id is refused (`config.py:150-152`, "duplicate metric id"). A grader that returns a key outside `inp.metrics` is recorded as outside (`runner.py:358`) and the pass fails with HB-GRD-004 (`errors.py:67`). `grade/formal.py` records no `pass_at_1` today.
  - ADR-0019 item 3 (accepted): "The formal grader records `pass_at_1` = 1 when its task's declared pass rule holds"; item 4 reads a missing value as not recorded. The ADR's own text already marks item 3's mechanism provisional on this request (`0019:49`). The board's primary is `pass_at_1` by id (`board.py:190,194`), so a new id would break it.
  - R-90 condition 6 says "Scenario-7 `pass_at_1` (ADR-0019 item 3) is untouched by this ruling; it stays the formal grader's." It fixes who records the metric; it does not freeze the catalog entry's bytes. R-90 condition 1 fixes one function for the narrowing so that three readers share one definition.
  - W1-G design 4.2 (`design/eval-catalog-0-7`, `b0987941`): owner = first of `[grader, *also_graded_by]` the task names; `metrics.yaml` line for `pass_at_1` gains `also_graded_by: [formal]`; W0 rev 3 section 7 records the clause provisional, the parameter `prop`, and the non-owner guard. RV-SIM W1-G 1 and RV-PAT W1-G 8 find `also_graded_by` the smallest correct form; RV-SIM 3 and RV-TA 8 raised the amendment and the unowned hunk; RV-PAT 1 found the HB-GRD-004 trip on G1; RV-PAT 2 found the unvalidated name; RV-PAT 6 found the owner flip in the task-changed fallback (`runner.py:318-321` names every catalog grader when the task hash does not match, so the owner is `correctness` and the NA lands in its folder).
- **Ruling:**
  - **(A) granted.** R-90 conditions 1 and 6 are amended as follows. **Condition 1** now reads: the single function `grade/runner.py: applicable(catalog, graders, prop)` carries two clauses and no third anywhere: (i) the `property:` narrowing as rev 3 states it, and (ii) owner resolution: a metric's owner for a task is the first of `[grader, *also_graded_by]` that the task's `graders` names; the metric is in the owner's set and in no other, so each (cell, metric) has exactly one row. Readiness and every other reader import this function, never a copy. **Condition 6** now reads: scenario-7 `pass_at_1` stays the formal grader's, recorded under `also_graded_by: [formal]` on the catalog entry; the entry's bytes change in 0.7 (so `catalog_hash` changes, which is why 0.7 is a version), and no 0.6 value moves (ADR-0019 item 3, "adds rows where none existed").
  - **Why this is an amendment of the letter and not of the purpose.** Condition 1's purpose was one definition for three readers; the owner clause lives in that one function, so the purpose is kept. Condition 6's purpose was that `pass_at_1` for a formal task is the formal grader's number, not a correctness NA; `also_graded_by: [formal]` is how the catalog says so. RV-SIM 3 read "untouched" as the entry's bytes; the ruling meant the owner, and says so now.
  - **(B) refused.** It defers an accepted ADR item to 0.8 to avoid one optional key, and it leaves G2 un-rankable through E1 for no measured saving. ADR-0019 item 4 keeps a missing value harmless, which is why (B) is safe, not why it is right.
  - **(C) refused.** A second id for one quantity is two definitions (DM7), and the board's primary reads `pass_at_1` by id; RV-SIM 1 rejects it on the same ground.
- **Conditions:**
  1. **One owner by construction, proven through a real pass (RV-PAT 1; W0 rev 3 section 7 non-owner guard).** `formal.grade_cell` emits `pass_at_1` only when `"pass_at_1" in inp.metrics`; a G1-shaped task (graders `formal` and `correctness`) through a real `run_pass` records one `pass_at_1` row owned by `correctness` and no HB-GRD-004; a G2-shaped task (`formal, drift, cost, process`) records one row owned by `formal`. Both tests are red against an unguarded `grade_cell` and against today's `applicable`. X-F owns the clause and T-R3..T-R5 (W0 rev 3; RV-TA 8 is closed by this assignment, and W1-G's test list moves them to X-F's file).
  2. **The key is validated at once, not at a second user (RV-SIM 1, RV-PAT 2).** `config.validate_catalog` checks each `also_graded_by` name is a grader module, differs from `grader`, and has no repeats, in X-A1's seam lines; a misspelt name is a catalog error, never a silent "no owner". The design's `simplify:` marker on deferred validation is withdrawn; the marker may stay for "one user" only.
  3. **The reader sweep is complete and written down (RV-PAT 2).** The DM15 table in W1-G 3 names `config.validate_catalog`, the task-changed fallback (`runner.py:318-321`) and `pack_improvement.py:491` with "unchanged because" or the change. For the fallback: when the task hash does not match, every catalog grader is named, so the owner is the entry's `grader` (`correctness`) and the `task changed` NA lands in its evidence folder; the amendment text in 4.2 says so, and one test pins the folder (RV-PAT 6). No reader widens.
  4. **The amendment text has one home.** The dispatch-rule amendment of R-90 condition 5 (W1-G 4.2's quoted paragraph) carries both clauses and the fallback sentence; W0 section 7 drops "provisional" on conditions 1 and 6 and cites R-95; ADR-0019's item-3 note (`0019:49`) is updated from "provisional on the request" to "R-95: `also_graded_by: [formal]`" by X-G1 in the `metrics.yaml` commit that adds the key.
  5. **Sequencing stands as the Coordinator set it.** X-G1 adds `also_graded_by` to `metrics.yaml` only after this ruling (now); X-F lands the clause before X-G1's catalog is graded under; the `pass_rule` semantics (W1-G 4.3) are untouched by this ruling, and RV-PAT 4's precedence order is W1-G's to fix at its gate.
- **What reverses it:** a second metric needing `also_graded_by` with a different precedence than "first named" (then the list is a rule, not a key, and comes back as a request); the formal grader ceasing to be a task grader (then `pass_at_1` for scenario 7 needs a new home, ADR-0019 item 3 by amendment). After catalog 0.7 freezes, any change here is a catalog version.

- request: req-01M41E37FGK5CRZ7NCK3RA20JV · ruled by: owner-fable · at: 2026-10-03T18:03:59Z

### Ruling 96 — DR-10 (W1-H's DR-H1): (A). correction.method=holm sizes and levels the verdict interval at alpha/m, Holm's first step, so verdicts are a subset of Holm's rejections; (B) step-down refused (cross-verdict dependency; no interval for the MDE comparison); holm is registrable and not a silent alias: alpha_per_test is the one definition, disclosed at registration and in section 3. The R-93 None state (hidden-test agreement not recorded) is inside R-93 read with IO, no separate ruling. ADR-0020 s2 'plan seed' -> seed_for: the Coordinator writes Amendment 1 in the rev-3 form

- **Recorded as DR-10** (W1-H labels it DR-H1; the campaign sequence continues from DR-9, R-95). Ruled from the worktree `owner/ruling-r96` at main `f41d2f20`. Three items: the `holm` request, the R-93 flag, and the ADR-0020 s2 amendment author.
- **Facts, Verified by me on 2026-10-03:**
  - ADR-0020 s2: the verdict is per (property, harness, comparison), from a task-stratified paired bootstrap interval "at the pre-registered level and correction", and the rule is interval-only: lower > 0 -> `better`, upper < 0 -> `worse`, inside (-MDE, +MDE) -> `no difference >= MDE`, else `inconclusive (underpowered)`. Alternatives: "McNemar test p-values as the verdict: rejected; the verdict needs an interval to compare with the MDE" (`0020:49`).
  - The spec's normative multiplicity numbers are Bonferroni: "alpha = 0.05 / 45 (Bonferroni over the campaign's 45 tests) -> 115 pairs" (`enterprise-evaluation.md:322,463,471`); "Holm over 45 tests" appears once, in an illustrative report line marked `[illustrative values]` (`:685`). W0 rev 3 s8 names the enum `correction {method: bonferroni|holm|none, m}` (`eval-seam-contracts.md:381`). W1-H (`2a9faa3c`) sizes and levels `holm` at `alpha/m` (`eval-power-verdicts.md:89,155,252`) and cut a Holm step-down on the ladder (`:151`).
  - R-93 condition 1: the line is read from `readiness.hidden_test_disagreements(...)` after the pass completes and "renders nothing when n = 0". W1-H's state table (`:188`) adds: `None` (the reader failed or was not run) -> `<p class="warn" data-kind="hidden-test-agreement-not-recorded">`, flagged as R-H3 (`:418`); its pilot refuses a `None` list with HB-USR-002 (F13, `:210`).
  - ADR-0020 s2 says "streams from `stats.rng(plan seed, key)`" (`0020:39`); W0 rev 3 s8 replaced the plan seed with `seed_for(...)` = `int(sha256(...).hexdigest()[:15], 16)` (W1-H `:72,:312`). W0 rev 3 recorded the other amendment notes of this wave as appended notes authored with rev 3 by the Coordinator (`eval-seam-contracts.md:661`; commit `d261d778`), decision text unchanged; ADR-0017's is W1-D's by R-94.
- **Ruling 1, the request: (A) granted; (B) refused.**
  - Under ADR-0020 the verdict is an interval compared with the MDE, and an interval has one level. Holm's first step is `alpha/m`; every later step is more liberal. So an interval at level `1 - alpha/m` is the one interval that is never more liberal than any Holm step, and a verdict read from it (`better`, `worse`) is a subset of Holm's rejections: nothing is called certain that Holm would not also call certain. That is the conservative direction for a campaign whose verdicts are the product.
  - (B) is refused for two reasons that stand independently. First, a step-down orders p-values across the m tests of one campaign, so one verdict's label would depend on the other verdicts' data: a cross-verdict dependency that ADR-0020's per-(property, harness, comparison) grain does not have and that the pre-registration (one MDE, one level per property) does not describe. Second, a step-down yields rejections, not an interval; the MDE comparison that gives `no difference >= MDE` and `inconclusive (underpowered)` their meaning needs the interval, which is why the ADR rejected p-value verdicts. Simultaneous intervals compatible with Holm exist in the literature but are uninformative for the rejected tests (Inferred, recalled: Strassburger and Bretz 2008; not opened here, and not load-bearing: the two reasons above carry the refusal).
  - **`holm` is accepted as a registrable name, and it is not a silent alias.** The enum stays as W0 rev 3 wrote it (the spec's own illustration uses the word). `alpha_per_test` is the one definition (DM7): `alpha/m` for `bonferroni` and `holm`, `alpha` for `none`. Two names mapping to one function is not two definitions; two names that could diverge would be, so condition 2 pins them together.
- **Conditions (ruling 1):**
  1. **Disclosure at both ends.** The power output and the verdict view carry `alpha_per_test` and a `level_rule` string, `"alpha/m (Bonferroni; Holm's first step)"` when the method is `holm`, and report section 3's legend prints it beside the level, so a reader never believes a step-down ran. The registration confirmation (P1, EV: "multiplicity correction") shows the same sentence before the hash is taken; what the operator registered is what the report says.
  2. **One function, pinned.** `test_holm_is_sized_like_bonferroni` (W1-H `:252`) stays, and a second row asserts the verdict interval's endpoints under `holm` equal those under `bonferroni` for the same seed and pairs (the sizing test alone would let the verdict level drift). Mutant: `holm` mapped to `alpha` must fail both.
  3. **`prereg.correction.method` is recorded as written.** A registered `holm` is never rewritten to `bonferroni` in the record (the record is the operator's statement); the effective rule is a derived field (condition 1), never a stored second value.
- **Ruling 2, the R-93 flag: inside R-93, no separate ruling.** R-93's own words are "never silently resolved either way (IO1; EV-14)" and condition 1's "renders nothing when n = 0" speaks of a count that was read. A reader that did not run has no count; rendering nothing would show it as "no disagreement", which is exactly the silent resolution R-93 forbids, and the pack's standing rule is that a measurement path "degrades to 'not recorded', never to a plausible wrong number" (IO). So the third state is R-93 read with IO, not an extension. It is recorded here as a clarification of R-93 condition 1: three states, list non-empty -> the warning with count and ids; empty list -> no element; `None` -> the `hidden-test-agreement-not-recorded` element with the reason, no count, no ids. Conditions: the two elements never share a `data-kind` and the not-recorded one never carries `data-count`; EVU-4 holds (no campaign -> no section, so no element of either kind); `section.built` records `hidden_disagreements: "not recorded"` (W1-H `:408`); the pilot gate's HB-USR-002 on `None` stands (a pilot stops, a report warns). `test_r93_not_recorded_when_reader_failed` (`:359`) is the red test; R-H3 closes.
- **Ruling 3, the ADR-0020 s2 note: the Coordinator writes it.** ADR-0020 *Amendment 1*, in the form W0 rev 3 used for ADR-0014/0018/0019 (`eval-seam-contracts.md:661`): a header bullet "**Amended (2026-10-03, W0 rev 3 s8; R-96):** `stats.rng(plan seed, key)` reads `stats.rng(seed_for(prereg_hash, property, harness, (ref, treat)), key)`; the seed is derived, 60 bits, never stored in the plan. See 'Amendment 1'." plus the one-paragraph section; the decision text is not rewritten. It lands with the next W0 revision or its own docs commit, before X-H1's first commit, so X-H1 codes against an ADR that says what W0 says. W1-H's "not mine to edit" (`:52`) is correct.
- **What reverses ruling 1:** a request carrying a Holm-compatible interval construction that yields an MDE-comparable interval per verdict without cross-verdict dependency, with its reference cases; or P1 registering `none` with a per-property MDE argument, which is a registration decision, not a method change. After a pre-registration is hashed, the method is frozen for that campaign.

- request: req-01M41EPSB7E91C4APYT1QGN3FV · ruled by: owner-fable · at: 2026-10-03T18:09:46Z

### Ruling 97 — DR-L1 (W1-L): (A). hallucinated_symbol_errors is the number of distinct unresolved references to the vendored API in the final tree inside the blast radius, measured by the grading pass (source D), never a trajectory count; (B) refused (no tool output extracted, and a tried-then-fixed count is a second quantity). The bench does have a grading-pass build.log (correctness.py:313-355) but Python's compileall cannot name a missing member, so the Python producer is W1-L's static resolver and a compiled-language producer is the build log, not built in E4 (NA, never 0). Resolver failure is NA, never 0. The spec's EV-5 gets an appended note, written by W1-L; ADR-0019 needs none; the catalog anchor_note drops 'build-log errors' (X-G1)

- **Recorded as DR-L1** (W1-L's label; the campaign sequence continues from DR-10, R-96). Ruled from the worktree `owner/ruling-r97` at main `a49c56da`. One item: the meaning and producer of `hallucinated_symbol_errors`.
- **Facts, Verified by me on 2026-10-03:**
  - The trajectory carries no tool output. `ToolCall` is `{native_ordinal, name, tool_class, start, end, ok, outcome_code}` (`src/harness_bench/telemetry/__init__.py:61-69`); `tool_call_rows` adds nothing (`telemetry/normalize.py:177-180`). The Claude Code extractor sees native `tool_result` blocks only to close the call (`telemetry/claude_code.py:101-104,131`). W1-L's G1 is correct: option B has no source today, and SR-L1 (targets) would not supply it either; it needs result text.
  - **The bench does have a build log, and W1-L's "no source" is too broad.** `grade/correctness.py:313-355` (`build_and_suite_clean`) writes `<out_dir>/build/build.log` in the grading pass: for `unittest`/`pytest` tasks the step is `python -m compileall -q .` (`:331`), which reports syntax errors only and can never name a missing member; for `dotnet` tasks it is `dotnet build` (`:333-334`), whose compiler errors do name a missing member. So EV-5's "build-log errors" has a real source for a compiled-language task and none for a Python task. The proposal that EV-5 inherited the phrase from says "D: build log parse" (`docs/proposals/enterprise-production-portfolio.html:183`), and the catalog carries `source: [D]` (`docs/design/eval-catalog-0-7.md:145`): the spec always meant the grading pass, never the trajectory.
  - NG1 (`tkem/cachetools`) and NG2 (`tomli`) are Python (`eval-property-tasks.md` section 2, section 7.3-7.4); neither declares `deliverable.build` (section 5.2). The W1-L design already names the one meaning it wants: "members that survive in the final tree" (section 7.1).
- **Ruling: (A) granted, with the meaning fixed; (B) refused.**
  - `hallucinated_symbol_errors` is **the number of distinct references, in the final tree inside `blast_radius`, to a member of the task's vendored API that the real library does not define** (a dotted name, or `lib.Cls.__init__:kw` for an unknown keyword, as section 7.1). It is a grading-pass measure (source D) over the deliverable, never a trajectory count. A name the agent defines itself is not a reference; dynamic access is a recorded residual, not an error.
  - (B) is refused on two independent grounds. The extraction has no tool output (G1), and adding it is a telemetry and identity change the request itself names. And even with it, a count of what the agent tried and then fixed is a different quantity from what survives in the deliverable; recording both under one id would be two definitions of one quantity (DM7). What EV-5 wants to know is whether the guess reached the deliverable; the trajectory question is `verified_before_use` (SR-L1), which stays as W1-L wrote it.
- **Conditions:**
  1. **One meaning, one producer per language, named in the strategy.** For a Python task the producer is W1-L's static resolver (section 7.1): `ast` collection in the radius, a `python -S` child with `sys.path` = the vendored path only, `hasattr` / `inspect.signature` against the real library. For a compiled-language task the producer is the correctness grader's `build.log`, compiler errors naming a missing member. **That path is not built in E4** (no compiled no-guessing task exists); such a task scores NA `hallucinated_symbol_errors: not built for <runner>`, never 0. `noguess.py`'s module docstring states both sentences.
  2. **Never degrades to 0 (IO).** If the resolver child cannot import the vendored library, times out, or a file in the radius does not parse, the metric is NA with the reason (`resolver failed: <first stderr line>` · `syntax error: <path>`), never 0. Red test named: `test_hse_resolver_failure_is_na` (X-LG), beside W1-L's section 15 tests.
  3. **The words follow the meaning.** Report, catalog and task files say "unresolved vendored-API references in the final tree"; "build-log errors" is retired from the catalog's `anchor_note` for this metric (`eval-catalog-0-7.md:145`, X-G1 at its next catalog edit, inside the 0.7.dev window; the anchor `[5, 0]` stays provisional per W1-G U2) and from the report wording the request names.
  4. **The discrimination record is the measurement.** NG1 naive = 2, `v-hallucinated` = 1, `v-kw` = 1, NG2 as declared, stay Inferred until X-NG's first strategy run; W1-L section 7.1 and section 16 D6 drop "provisional on DR-L1" and cite R-97. Residual: a one-line deliverable with no reference to the library (the agent reimplements it) scores 0 here and fails the hidden tests; the pair reads correctly, and the resolver never infers intent.
- **Amendment notes, who writes them.**
  - **The spec needs one, W1-L writes it.** `docs/specs/enterprise-evaluation.md` EV-5, third acceptance line: an appended note under EV-5, decision text unchanged (the W0 rev 3 form, `eval-seam-contracts.md:661`): "`hallucinated_symbol_errors` is measured by the grading pass over the final tree: unresolved references to the vendored API (a static resolver for Python; the build log for a compiled language). R-97." W1-L appends it in its gate revision (the W1-D / ADR-0017 pattern, R-94); the Coordinator carries the ruling into W0 rev 4's "rulings this doc reads" line.
  - **ADR-0019 needs none.** Item 2 lists the metric id and sets its kind, source and weight; it defines no meaning and nothing in it is contradicted. Its Evidence line is not touched.
- **Reversibility:** high, as the request says: the id, kind, `better` and anchor are unchanged; only the producer and the wording move. If a compiled-language no-guessing task is ever authored, condition 1 already names its producer.

- request: req-01M41GWNS06F362RJ78XB7AXY5 · ruled by: owner-fable · at: 2026-10-03T18:54:39Z

### Ruling 98 — DR-E1 (Coordinator, W1-E SR-E1 1): (A). The discrimination record body drops run_id and grading_id; the committed record is a pure function of its key and measured content, so a retry at an unchanged key is equal bytes and ADR-0016 s2a's one meaning of already-done holds with no subset-compare exception; (B) refused (two definitions of one predicate). The run link moves to the local git-ignored runs/<run>/discrimination-link.json, written only after created-or-equal, never after HB-RDY-010; reconciliation never degrades to a pass. Grain restated: one file is one discrimination result of one key; a later trial confirms it or is HB-RDY-010. ADR-0016 Amendment 1 and the W0 s6 rewrite are the Coordinator's with W0 rev 6, before X-E's first record

- **Recorded as DR-E1** (the Coordinator's label; the campaign sequence continues from DR-L1, R-97). Ruled from the worktree `owner/ruling-r98` (base `9879623f`, main at `f82a816c`). One item: whether the discrimination record's body holds `run_id` and `grading_id`.
- **Facts, Verified by me on 2026-10-03:**
  - ADR-0016 §4: the record "holds ... the synthetic run id and grading id", and the copy of the score sets is "identified by `(run_id, grading_id)`" for read-time reconciliation, which runs only "when the referenced run exists locally". §2a: `create_once` treats equal bytes as a no-op and different bytes as a determinism defect, and "the same compare-and-refuse rule is the idempotency rule of the ledger commands (§1), so there is one definition of 'already done'". §7: the campaign "references, never copies"; records are referenced by key, not by run.
  - RV-DS W0 F2 (`docs/design/reviews/eval-review-ds.md:29`, major): with the ids in the body "two productions never have equal bytes", so every legitimate retry at an unchanged key is HB-LED-007, a false determinism defect. W0 rev 2 answered with option (a): read the key first and compare a field subset; W0 rev 5 §6 widens the subset to `scores`, `expected`, `readiness_failures`, `probe`, `variants` and says "until ruled, option (a) stands; X-E codes option (a)".
  - W1-E §4.2-4.3 (`design/eval-discriminate` `549f7bc4`): the body drops the ids; the writer records `runs/<run_id>/discrimination-link.json` (`bench-discrimination-link/1`: record path, `grading_id`, timings, counts), local and git-ignored; reconciliation (HB-RDY-004) uses the newest link whose `record` equals the record's path; no link or no run folder prints `reconciled: no (no local run)` and neither fails nor passes. Section 4.3 is marked provisional on SR-E1 with option (a) as its fallback.
  - No record exists: `bench/discrimination/` is absent on main and in this tree; `create_once` is not yet in `src/harness_bench` (X-B1, E1); no module under `src/` reads a discrimination record. Reversibility is high today and low after the first record is committed, as the request says.
- **Ruling: (A) granted. The body drops `run_id` and `grading_id`; (B) refused.**
  - The ADR already states the invariant that decides this: one definition of "already done", bytes-equal (§2a). A body that holds a per-production id cannot satisfy it, and option (b) keeps the contradiction alive by adding a subset-compare exception beside the bytes rule: two definitions of one predicate (DM7). (A) removes the exception rather than maintaining it.
  - The ids' only use in the ADR is reconciliation, which already requires the run to exist locally; on every other clone the ids are a dangling reference inside a committed file. The link belongs beside its referent, in `runs/`, which is where W1-E puts it. The committed record becomes a pure function of its key and its measured content; its provenance is the key, the git commit that adds it, and the local link where the run exists. Nothing the campaign reads on a fresh clone is lost, because nothing on a fresh clone could follow the ids.
  - **The grain is restated, not changed in kind.** ADR-0016 §4 says "one file is exactly one discrimination trial"; with the ids gone it reads "one file is exactly one discrimination **result** of one task version under one engine identity on one platform; a later trial at the same key confirms it (equal bytes) or exposes a determinism defect (HB-RDY-010), and is never a second record". The amendment note says so in those words.
- **Conditions:**
  1. **A link is written only after agreement.** `discriminate` writes `runs/<run_id>/discrimination-link.json` after `create_once` returns created or equal, never after HB-RDY-010, so every link points at a run whose grading agreed with the record when the link was written. Red test (X-E, beside T-E6): a differing re-run leaves the stored bytes unchanged **and** writes no link.
  2. **HB-RDY-010 before HB-LED-007, as W1-E §4.3.** `discriminate` reads the key first only to name the first differing metric, the stored value and the new value; the two codes are never both raised for one write. HB-LED-007 keeps its one meaning (bytes differ, in `create_once`) and is the backstop, not the message.
  3. **Reconciliation never degrades to a pass.** "Newest link" is by the link file's own `recorded_at` (the writer's stamp, W0 §6 `stamp`), and with no link, no run folder, or a link whose run has no completed grading pass, `readiness` prints `reconciled: no (<reason>)` and neither fails nor passes (IO). The reconciliation diff itself stays ADR-0016 §4's: a difference against the run's current pass is HB-RDY-004.
  4. **No code path keeps option (a).** X-E codes (A) from its first commit; if any option-(a) compare-subset branch has been written on its tree, it is removed before the first record is committed, not kept as a fallback (HYG-A). W1-E §4.3 drops "provisional (seam SR-E1)" and its fallback paragraph in its gate revision.
  5. **`bench campaign verify` is unaffected and stays so.** The record's name is key-derived, not a hash of its bytes, and `verify`'s discrimination check is `git status --porcelain bench/discrimination` (ADR-0016 §8). No reader of the record body is added by this ruling.
- **Amendment note, who writes it.** ADR-0016 needs one, appended as *Amendment 1* with the decision text unchanged, in the rev-3/rev-4 form (`eval-seam-contracts.md:728,781`): §4's "the synthetic run id and grading id" is withdrawn, the grain sentence above is recorded, the local link file is named as the reconciliation path, and §2a is noted to hold for the record with no exception. **The Coordinator writes it with W0 rev 6**, as the request offers, and rewrites W0 §6's "A second production at the same key" paragraph to conditions 1-3 in the same revision. Both land before X-E's first record commit; the Leader orders the merge so.
- **Reversibility:** high, because this ruling is issued before any record exists. After the first committed record the body form is frozen by the schema name (`bench-discrimination/1`); a later change is a new schema, never an edit at an existing key.

- request: req-01M41J1E3PDYAG1WTAGH004MZ8 · ruled by: owner-fable · at: 2026-10-03T19:20:59Z

### Ruling 99 — DR-S2: S2's probe class avoids bottle's pickle signed-cookie path (option a); done-when preserved; (c) refused, (b) the fallback

DR-S2 (Leader, S2 probe class; the X-I-S2 spike was stopped by a safety classifier): (a). S2 keeps bottle at `cbd569c4` and measures a latent security requirement whose probes never touch the signed-cookie deserialization path. No S2 artefact — prompt, probe, payload, variant, reference or naive solution, or check — ever constructs a pickle gadget or a validly-signed (forged) cookie, and the check never imports `bottle` or `pickle`. (b) a different base is the fallback if (a) cannot discriminate; (c) dropping S2 is refused here (it needs the operator and reduces the ten-property-task done-when). The done-when is preserved: S2 stays one of the ten property tasks with a discrimination record.

- **Recorded as DR-S2** (the Leader's delegation; the campaign sequence continues from DR-E1, R-98). Ruled from the worktree `owner/ruling-r99` (base `b466633b`). One item: what S2 measures so no artefact constructs a deserialization exploit or a forged-cookie procedure, while S2 still discriminates a secure solution from an insecure one.

- **Facts, Verified by me on 2026-10-03:**
  - W1-I §12 (`docs/design/eval-security-tasks.md:440-457`) cuts S2 to base, pin, licence, tenancy and probe *classes* only; ids, payloads, the latent sentence, evidence paths and the prompt are fixed only after an S2 spike in E4, each probe proven live by a defect variant (§5.5). The rev-2 classes are injection (SQL), path traversal, authorization bypass (cross-team), secret leak (the cookie signing secret), and forged-session. Base `bottlepy/bottle` @ `cbd569c4…`, MIT, single module, imports under `python -S`, 363 upstream tests of which 6 fail under `-S`, no PASS_TO_PASS.
  - The signed-cookie path is pickle-based. The stopped-spike note (`docs/notes/spike-s2-bottle-cookie-reads.md`, Verified reads at the pin) records `bottle.py:2978` `cookie_encode` does `pickle.dumps` before signing; `:1190` `get_cookie` calls `pickle.loads` **after** the signature check; `:1803` the `set_cookie` docstring warns a leaked secret is code execution on unpickle. W1-I §12 item 2 had carried these as Inferred; the note promotes them to Verified. No S2 probe, variant or run exists; the spike is stopped and the Leader will not route around the classifier.
  - W1-I §12 item 2 already requires, before E4: (a) the check gets a valid cookie only through the app's own login route **or** an HMAC re-implemented in the check, never by importing bottle; (b) forged-cookie probes send inert bytes, never a pickle gadget; (c) a defect variant where the secret leaks and a forged cookie is accepted; and if (a)-(c) cannot all hold, the base or session changes (an Owner question for E4). RV-SEC's W1-I review finding 2 (`docs/design/reviews/eval-review-sec-w1i.md`) is the source, severity major, disposition accepted.
  - The kickoff done-when needs all ten property tasks with discrimination records, S2 among them; (c) would reduce it and needs the operator (stated in the brief `docs/coordination/eval-wave2-e234/x-i-s2.md`).

- **Ruling: (a) granted. (c) refused here. (b) is the named fallback.**
  - The signature check precedes `pickle.loads` (`:1190`), so a cookie with no valid signature is **rejected before it is unpickled**. That ordering is what makes (a) sound: a tamper-refusal probe can send unsigned or wrong-signature plaintext bytes and the host never unpickles attacker-controlled bytes. Discrimination does not need a forged valid cookie — it needs the app to *refuse* an invalid one. The insecure shape (an unsigned/plain session cookie, or a cookie whose signature is not verified, or a client-supplied team id trusted without integrity) accepts the inert tampered bytes; the secure shape refuses them. Secure-vs-insecure is separated with zero exploit construction.
  - Cross-team authorization, secret non-disclosure, SQL injection and path traversal are all latent-requirement classes that never touch deserialization. They carry S2's discrimination on their own; tamper-refusal adds the session-integrity dimension without the pickle path.
  - W1-I §12 item 2(a)'s alternative "an HMAC re-implemented in the check" is **withdrawn**: re-implementing bottle's `cookie_encode` reconstructs a sign-a-pickle (forged-cookie) procedure, which is exactly what no artefact may contain. A valid session is obtained only by driving the app's own login route.

- **Conditions:**
  1. **Probe classes for S2:** injection (SQL), path traversal, authorization bypass (cross-team tenancy), secret non-disclosure (the `BENCHCANARY-S2-<hex>` signing secret never appears in a response, log or error — an inert scan as S1's leak probes, run for both teams), and **tamper-refusal** (the app must reject an unsigned or mis-signed session cookie). The rev-2 **forged-session** class is replaced by tamper-refusal.
  2. **Inert bytes only.** Every tamper/session probe sends unsigned or wrong-signature plaintext; no payload is a pickle gadget or a validly-signed cookie. The host never unpickles attacker bytes, because the signature check at `:1190` rejects them first. No probe demonstrates code execution.
  3. **No forged-cookie procedure, no `pickle`, no `bottle` import in the check.** A valid session comes only from the app's own login route; item 2(a)'s HMAC-re-implementation alternative is withdrawn. Extend S1's §5.9 no-build/import test for S2 so `check.py` importing `pickle` (and the existing `socket`/`subprocess`/`urllib`/`http` set) reddens, and add `pickle`/`cPickle` to the argv/word denylist; red-first on a fixture check that imports `pickle`.
  4. **Secret-leak consequence is prose only.** The canary stays the signing secret and the probe scans for its disclosure with inert bytes; the "leaked secret → code execution" consequence is recorded in `evidence.md` and the spike note as the reason the class is high-impact, and is never demonstrated or constructed.
  5. **Discrimination, each probe live.** The reference refuses unsigned/tampered cookies, enforces cross-team authz and secret non-disclosure, and is injection/traversal safe; the naive accepts an unsigned/tampered session cookie, or discloses the secret, or permits cross-team access, or is injectable/traversable. Each probe is proven live by a defect variant (§5.5), and **no variant constructs a pickle gadget or a validly-signed forged cookie** — §12 item 2(c)'s "a forged cookie is accepted" variant is realised as the naive accepting inert unsigned/tampered bytes.
  6. **Done-when preserved.** S2 remains one of the ten property tasks with a discrimination record; there is no scope reduction, so (c) is not taken.
  7. **Fallback (b), bounded.** If the E4 S2 spike shows (a)'s probes cannot discriminate a secure from an insecure solution without touching deserialization, S2 moves to a different MIT/Apache base or a non-pickle session (a new Owner request then, not this ruling). (c) — dropping S2 — becomes available only with the operator's explicit agreement to reduced ten-property-task coverage.

- **Who amends:**
  - **W1-I §12 open item 2** is amended by **X-I (session `x-is2-e1e4`)** as **Erratum 2**, citing R-99, in the commit that supersedes the stopped spike note — the Erratum-1 precedent (X-I authored it while building S1). The decision text of §12 is unchanged; the erratum records: classes per condition 1; item 2(a)'s HMAC alternative withdrawn (login route only); inert-bytes-only; the `pickle` check-import denylist; tamper-refusal replaces forged-session.
  - **The X-I-S2 brief** (`docs/coordination/eval-wave2-e234/x-i-s2.md`) step 1 item 2 is amended by **the Coordinator** to the login-route-only / inert-bytes / `pickle`-denylist / tamper-refusal rule, so X-I spikes and authors S2 against a brief that matches this ruling. Both amendments land before X-I authors S2 in E4; the Leader routes the RV-SEC (hard veto) and RV-TA review of the redesigned spike note, per the X-I-S2 brief step 1.3.

- **Operator: told, not asked.** (c) is refused, so no operator agreement is required now. One line goes in the next scheduled operator report: "S2's probe class was redesigned to avoid bottle's pickle signed-cookie path after a safety classifier stopped the original spike; S2 is retained as one of the ten property tasks with a discrimination record, no scope reduction; the base may change (option b) only if the E4 spike shows (a) cannot discriminate. R-99." The operator may revisit the base at that point; it is not a block.

- **Reversibility:** high. No S2 artefact exists — the spike is stopped, no probe, variant or run. W1-I §12 is open text and the brief is proposed. Reversible by editing until X-I authors S2 in E4; low only after the first S2 task files and discrimination record are committed.

- request: req-01M41T6XB206JRN9V1AGFN92X9 · ruled by: owner-fable · at: 2026-10-03T21:20:15Z

### Ruling 100 — DR-K1 (W1-K): (B). A resume of a stopped run finishes the stop - launches nothing, records stopped for intent-without-outcome cells, archives, exits 3; the invariant is NoLaunchAfterStop (the existing launchAfterStop guard), not no-resume-after-stop; D-K9's StopCrashed liveness disjunct withdrawn; HB-RUN-008 re-pointed to the stopped run's exit-3 reason; (A) refused

DR-K1 (W1-K, `req-01M41V2MC1RTCBB4APR3TSXEYP`, overdue; fallback A built provisionally): **(B)**. A resume of a stopped run runs in **finish-the-stop** mode: it launches nothing, reconciles every cell that has `launch_intent` and no outcome to `stopped`, archives, writes `run.stopped` if the crash fell before it, writes no `run.completed`, starts no grading pass, and exits 3 as a stop does. The invariant ADR-0021 §2 protects is **no launch after a stop** — the model already states it as the `launchAfterStop` guard on `WriteIntent` — not "no resume after a stop". (A) refused: it leaves cells non-terminal forever in a ledger whose own rule (§3) says terminal-but-not-archived is a reconciliation case, and it buys that hole by weakening four liveness properties (D-K9's `StopCrashed` disjunct), which is withdrawn.

- **Recorded as DR-K1** (the request carries no DR id; the campaign sequence continues from DR-S2, R-99). Ruled from the worktree `owner/ruling-r100` (base `86542f5b`). One item: after a crash between a stop row and the cells' `stopped` outcomes, is the run left with unfinished cells (A), or may a resume finish the stop without launching (B)?

- **Facts, Verified by me on 2026-10-03:**
  - ADR-0021 §2 bullet 2: "The run has a `run.stopped` or `stop` event: refused (the 'no launch after a stop' invariant)." §3: a cell is terminal iff its outcome transition is recorded; terminal-but-not-archived "is a reconciliation case, not a terminal-and-done one". §6: the operator sees every cell accounted for; Consequences: "every cell accounted for". §9: the model gains "no resume after a stop" as an invariant.
  - `engine.py:449-457` `_apply_stop` appends `run.stopped`, then `_kill_all("stop")`; the cells' `stopped` outcomes are written later by the worker threads (`engine.py:634, 654, 669`: `outcome = "stopped" if kill_reason == "stop"`, cause `None`, no SPEND). `_read_controls` appends `control.applied{stop, applied}` *before* `_apply_stop` (`:518-524`); an answered stop decision appends `decision.resolved{stop}` first (`:353-357`). So three crash windows leave cells with intent and no outcome (W1-K D-K4, Verified against the same lines). `procs.py:216-220`: the Job Object is unnamed and `KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`, so the engine's death ends every cell process; nothing survives to relaunch or to race.
  - `models/run_lifecycle.tla`: `WriteIntent` already guards `~stopApplied` and flags `launchAfterStop` (`:159-162`); `Resume` (`:552`) carries no stop guard today; `StopReachesTerminal == controlFile["stop"] ~> (stopApplied /\ \A c : intent[c] => outcome[c] # "none" /\ proc[c] # "running")` (`:776`); `Fairness` has `WF_vars(Resume)` and `WF_vars(ReconcileRecord(c))` (`:693-700`). W1-K's in-progress design (`eval-resume.md` on `design/eval-resume`, uncommitted) builds A: §3.1 step 3(a) refuses with HB-RUN-008 listing the residue; D-K9 adds `StopCrashed == engine = "down" /\ stopApplied` as a disjunct to four liveness properties; D-K10 states `NoResumeAfterStop` on `stopApplied`; §10 row "the engine dies mid-stop: cells without outcome; resume refused; alarm silent"; §12 names this request with fallback A.
  - W0 §11: `HB-RUN-008 resume refused: the run was stopped` (X-K1, E3). No `resume.py` exists; X-K1 is E3, so no code moves (reversibility high, as the request says).

- **Ruling: (B) granted; (A) refused.**
  - **The outcome is a function of rows, not a guess.** A cell with `launch_intent` and no outcome in a ledger that holds a stop row *before* the crash was killed by the stop (the stop row precedes the kill in `_apply_stop`, and kill-on-close finished what the kill began). The engine's own rule for that kill reason is `stopped`, cause `None`, no SPEND (`engine.py:634, 654, 669`). The resume applies the same rule from the same rows; nothing is recomputed that the live engine would not have written. That is the C2/C3/C5 discipline already accepted for `failed (coordinator crash)`, with the stop row choosing the branch.
  - **A leaves the ledger unable to reach a consistent state.** Under A the cells are "pending" by §3's definition on a run no engine may ever re-enter: `bench status` shows pending cells with no process forever, the alarm needs a stop exception (W1-K §10: "alarm silent (operator stop)"), the stopped cells' working copies are the only copy of their trees and are neither archived nor released to teardown, and §6's "every cell accounted for" is met only by a refusal message, never by the record. DM: history is the data; a hole that a deterministic rule could fill is a shadow of the record kept in an error string.
  - **A costs the model four weakened promises; B costs none.** D-K9 adds `StopCrashed` as a disjunct to `StopReachesTerminal`, `EndedCellsGetArchived`, `PromptedCellsEnd` and `ArchivedCellsGetGraded` so that TLC accepts a run whose stop never reaches terminal. Under B, with `WF_vars(Resume)` and `WF_vars(ReconcileRecord(c))` already in `Fairness`, `StopReachesTerminal` holds through a crash with no disjunct: the resume records the outcomes. A model that is weakened to match a design's hole is the wrong direction (IO: the model is the measurement of the design).
  - **B is not a second resume mode.** The engine after `_apply_stop` is already "launch nothing, drain, exit 3" (`_stop_launching`, `run_stopped`). A finish-the-stop resume restores `run_stopped` from the ledger before the loop and runs the same reconciliation with launching disabled: one flag read from rows, one classifier input, the same actions. The request's "second resume mode to test" is one parametrised test family (condition 5), which A needs too (W12's four params).

- **Conditions:**
  1. **ADR-0021 Amendment 1, the Coordinator writes it**, in the rev-3 form (`eval-seam-contracts.md:661`; R-96, R-98 precedent), with the D-K1/D-K2/D-K4/D-K5 corrections W1-K §12 already assigns to the Coordinator — one note, decision text unchanged. §2 bullet 2 reads: "The run has a stop row (D-K4's three): the resume runs in finish-the-stop mode — launches nothing, reconciles, archives, exits 3; the invariant is **no launch after a stop**." §9's "no resume after a stop" reads "no launch after a stop". It lands with W1-K's gate merge or before X-K1's first commit, whichever is first.
  2. **The model keeps its promises.** W1-K (owner of the model edit by its brief) renames the invariant `NoLaunchAfterStop == ~flags["launchAfterStop"]` (the flag exists; no new flag), `Resume` takes no `stopApplied` guard, and reconciliation under `stopApplied` records `stopped` (not `crashfail`/`crashbetween`) for intent-without-outcome cells. **D-K9 is withdrawn: no `StopCrashed` disjunct in any liveness property.** TLC output in the design must show the liveness configuration green *with* a crash after `ApplyStop` reachable (state count cited), and the seeded variant `launch_after_stop` (exists) **rejected**; the `resume_after_stop` variant is retired. D-K10 is restated on `launchAfterStop`.
  3. **Classifier: one input, not a second table.** `resume.classify(plan, rows)` reads the stop fact with D-K4's three-row predicate. When set: C2, C3, C5 and C6 record `stopped` (cause `None`, no SPEND, `phase` as today, `resume` object naming the segment and that the resume recorded it), C4 still redoes the snapshot first, C7 takes no action (a normal stop leaves never-launched cells unlaunched and unrecorded: `run.launch_stopped` is the record), C0 and C1 are unchanged. `run.launch_stopped` alone is **not** a stop (D-K4 stands). The resume writes `run.stopped` in its own segment when no `run.stopped` row exists (windows 1 and 2), then runs the engine loop with `run_stopped` restored: no `cell.launch_intent`, no `run.completed`, no grading pass, exit 3.
  4. **HB-RUN-008 is kept and re-pointed.** It is the stopped run's exit-3 reason, emitted by every finish-the-stop resume ("run is stopped: n cells recorded stopped, m archived, 0 launched"), and by an idempotent re-run with zero actions. It is no longer a refusal; §3.1 step 3's order becomes identity (b), verify (c), then the stop branch. The Coordinator updates the W0 §11 row's text in the same revision as condition 1; W1-K §9 says "confirmed, re-pointed".
  5. **Tests, red first (X-K1).** W12 becomes: three params (`control.applied{stop}` only / `decision.resolved{stop}` only / `run.stopped` only), each with cells in C2, C3, C5 and C6 states → every intent cell gains `cell.outcome{stopped}` with the `resume` object, each is archived, **no new `launch_intent`**, no `run.completed`, exit 3, `run.stopped` present exactly once; a fourth param `run.launch_stopped` only → launches; a fifth: the same ledger resumed twice is byte-identical after the second call (idempotent, zero actions, HB-RUN-008 text). `lifecycle.py`'s replay rule (W1-K §8) rejects a `cell.launch_intent` after a stop row, not a `run.resumed`.
  6. **Alarm and status need no stop exception.** `bench status --alarm-after` reads "cells pending" as before; after a finish-the-stop resume no cell is pending, so the alarm is silent by the record, not by a rule. If a crash lands mid-stop and nobody resumes, the alarm fires — that is correct: the operator asked for a stop and the ledger does not yet say it finished.

- **Who amends.** W1-K amends its own design in its gate revision (not yet gated): §1 D-K9 withdrawn and D-K10 restated; §3.1 step 3 and step 8; §3.2 the stop input; §4 W12; §5 the invariant rename and TLC rows; §9 HB-RUN-008 re-pointed; §10 row; §12 DR row closed with R-100. The Coordinator writes ADR-0021 Amendment 1 (condition 1) and the W0 §11 text (condition 4). X-K1 builds from the gated design; no code exists today.

- **Reversibility:** high, as the request says: no `resume.py`, no record, no row format changes; `stopped` is an existing outcome of the closed taxonomy. After X-K1 ships, reverting to A is a refusal branch and the four disjuncts — the same reversible edit in the other direction.

- request: req-01M41V2MC1RTCBB4APR3TSXEYP · ruled by: owner-fable · at: 2026-10-03T22:33:44Z

### Ruling 101 — DR-K2 (W1-K F-1): (a). The finish-the-stop resume runs the live engine's post-stop tail - grades the archived cells and writes run.completed, then exits 3 (engine.py:412-426: ended_whole ignores run_stopped). Supersedes R-100 condition 3's 'no run.completed, no grading pass'; everything else in R-100 stands; no model edit; (b) refused

DR-K2 (Leader relaying W1-K rev 1.1 finding F-1, `design/eval-resume` 315cf1d4 §5.3, §12): **(a)**. The finish-the-stop resume runs the live engine's own post-stop tail: it grades the archived cells and writes `run.completed` (with its `grading` field), then exits 3. **R-100 condition 3's clause "no `run.completed`, no grading pass" is superseded by this ruling**; every other word of R-100 stands. This is a new ruling, not an in-place clarification: R-100 recorded that clause as a condition, and the register's rule is that a ruling is never edited in place — a later one supersedes it and cites it.

- **Recorded as DR-K2** (the campaign sequence continues from DR-K1, R-100). Ruled from the worktree `owner/ruling-r101`. One item: which of (a) the resume grades as the engine does after a stop, (b) the resume does not grade and `bench grade` is the path, (c) other.

- **Facts, Verified by me on 2026-10-03 (`src/harness_bench/engine.py:412-437`):**
  - `ended_whole = not self.broken and not self.archive_failed` — a stop does **not** clear it. So after `_apply_stop` the live engine drains, runs `self.cfg.grade(self.cfg.run_dir)` under the heartbeat (`:414-416`), appends `run.completed{segment_heads, cells_ended, grading}` (`:423-426`), seals, and only then returns exit 3 because `run_stopped` is set (`:436`). The live stop's tail is therefore *drain → grade → `run.completed` → exit 3*, not *drain → exit 3*.
  - R-100's reasoning said "the engine after `_apply_stop` is already 'launch nothing, drain, exit 3'" and condition 3 drew "no `run.completed`, no grading pass" from it. The first clause was incomplete and the condition inherited the gap: it made the resumed stop's ledger differ from the live stop's in two rows, not one. W1-K rev 1.1 §3.1 step 8 says "differs only in the grading pass"; by the lines above it differed in `run.completed` too.
  - W1-K §5.3 F-1: the model keeps `GradeStart(p, "engine")` enabled on a stopped run, so `ArchivedCellsGetGraded` holds through a crash after `ApplyStop` only because the modelled resumed engine may grade; the code under R-100 condition 3 would not. D-K5 (W1-K §1): "complete iff a `run.completed` row follows the last `run.resumed` row", written precisely because a launch stop already produces `run.completed` with cells never launched.

- **Ruling: (a) granted; (b) refused; (c) not needed.**
  - **One ledger shape for one situation (DM7).** A stop finished live and a stop finished by a resume must leave the same rows: outcomes, archives, a grading pass, `run.completed`. Under (b) the same operator action yields two shapes depending on whether the engine happened to crash, and every reader (`views.load`'s `completed`, `bench status`, the report header, D-K5's rule) needs a second definition of "a stopped run ended". That is the two-definitions defect signature.
  - **The model and the code agree without an exception.** The model's resumed engine grades; under (a) so does the code. `ArchivedCellsGetGraded` holds in both for the same reason, and R-100's "no `StopCrashed` disjunct" stays satisfied with no model edit. (b) would have required either a model exception R-100 forbids or a model that proves a promise the code does not keep (IO: the model is the measurement).
  - **R-100's own principle decides it.** R-100 granted B because "B is not a second resume mode: the resume restores `run_stopped` from the ledger and runs the same path". The same path *includes* the tail. Condition 3 cut the tail short on a misread of the engine; correcting it makes the resume exactly the engine's post-stop behaviour, which is the smallest correct change (no new grading code, as W1-K §3.1 step 8 already states for the not-stopped branch).
  - **Grading a stopped run is already the engine's behaviour, not new risk.** Stopped cells are excluded from verdicts by their outcome, not by the absence of a grading pass; the grader sees them today on every live stop. `bench grade` (US-26) remains the re-run path for a grading pass that failed, as it is for a live run, not the primary path for a resumed stop.

- **Conditions:**
  1. **R-100 condition 3 reads, from here:** "... then runs the engine loop with `run_stopped` restored: no `cell.launch_intent`; the live tail as `engine.py:412-426`: drain, the grading pass when `cfg.grade` is set (a failed pass is recorded in `grading` and never costs the run), `run.completed` with `grading`, seal; exit 3." R-100 conditions 1, 2, 4, 5, 6 are unchanged. The HB-RUN-008 line gains the pass: "run is stopped: n cells recorded stopped, m archived, graded, 0 launched".
  2. **D-K5's completion rule is the one reader.** A finish-the-stop resume writes `run.resumed` first and `run.completed` last, so `complete iff run.completed follows the last run.resumed` holds and exit 3 still reports the stop; no second predicate.
  3. **Test (X-K1, red first), added to R-100 condition 5's family:** each of the three stop-window params asserts a grading pass ran (the `grading` object present in `run.completed`, or its `error_code` when the pass is made to fail), and `run.completed` appears exactly once after `run.resumed`; the idempotent re-run writes no second `run.completed`.
  4. **Model: no edit.** W1-K §5.3's F-1 paragraph is rewritten to "closed by R-101: the resumed engine grades, as the model already allows"; §3.1 step 8's stopped branch and §12's F-1 row cite R-101. W1-K writes this in its gate revision; the ADR-0021 Amendment 1 text (R-100 condition 1, the Coordinator's) is unaffected — §2's bullet never spoke of grading.

- **Reversibility:** high; no code exists. The correction removes two lines from a condition and adds none to the engine.

- request: req-01M42049A4NZ66MG3XNXBX10D6 · ruled by: owner-fable · at: 2026-10-03T23:03:46Z

### Ruling 102 — DR-K3 (W1-K reviews): item 1 (c) one predicate resume.has_work shared by resume and alarm, the stop row inside the definition of work (C7 cells are work only while no stop row exists; a crash in grading or archive is work) - (a) a stored outcome for never-launched cells and (b) an alarm-side stop exception refused; R-100 condition 6 restated. Item 2: ntfy push is the primary channel and required for an unattended run, toast optional at the drill, bounded egress accepted, edge-triggered wrapper with a delivery log and never the topic; ADR-0021 s7 amended; the operator sets HB_ALARM_NTFY_TOPIC and runs the drill before E5

DR-K3 (Leader relaying W1-K's reviews: SRE 1, 2, 4, 5, 6; PAT 2, 3; SIM 3). Two items, one ruling. **Item 1 — "pending": (c).** One predicate, `resume.has_work(plan, rows)`, the negation of W1-K §3.1 step 4, read by both `resume_run` and `alarm.check`; inside it, a cell with no `launch_intent` counts as work **only while no stop row exists** (D-K4's predicate). (a) a terminal outcome for never-launched cells is refused; (b) an alarm-side stop exception is refused. R-100 condition 6 is restated: the alarm and the resume need no exception *of their own* because they share one definition of work. **Item 2 — the channel: ntfy push is the primary channel and is required for an unattended run**; the toast is optional and joins at the drill; one HTTPS POST of `{run id, HB-ALM code, cause, age}` is accepted egress. The wrapper alerts on the edge, never prints the topic, and keeps a local delivery log. ADR-0021 §7's "a Windows toast at minimum" is amended. The operator must set `HB_ALARM_NTFY_TOPIC` and run the drill before E5 — a line for the Leader's next report.

- **Recorded as DR-K3** (the campaign sequence continues from DR-K2, R-101). Ruled from the worktree `owner/ruling-r102`.

- **Facts, Verified by me on 2026-10-03:**
  - W1-K rev 1.1 §6.2: "Pending = plan cells with no `cell.outcome`"; "no stop exception (R-100 condition 6)"; HB-ALM-001 fires when pending > 0 and the lock is free. §3.2 C7 under a stop: no action, never-launched cells stay unrecorded (R-100 condition 3: "`run.launch_stopped` is the record"). `engine.py:445` writes `run.launch_stopped` and no cell row. So after every stop with an unlaunched cell, live or resumed, the lock is free and pending > 0: HB-ALM-001 every 15 minutes for the window (SRE 2, PAT 2, independently). R-100 condition 6's premise "no cell is pending after a finish-the-stop" was wrong for C7 cells.
  - SRE 1: §3.1 step 4 defines "nothing to do" as no pending cell, `run.completed` after the last `run.resumed`, and no outcome-without-`cell.archived`; §6.2's pending ignores the last two. A crash in the grading pass or between the last outcome and `cell.archived` / `run.completed` leaves zero pending cells and a free lock: the resume has work, the alarm is silent for good. `engine.py:412-426` (R-101): grading and `run.completed` follow the loop with no pending cell.
  - ADR-0021 §7: the alarm fires "while cells are pending"; the channel is "chosen at `/design-slice`" and must turn a non-zero exit into "a notification the operator sees away from the terminal: a Windows toast at minimum, and a push to the operator's phone if one is configured", proven by a drill the operator acknowledges; `bench campaign register` refuses a multi-night grid until the drill is recorded. W1-K §6.3 chose toast primary, ntfy "optional, off by default". Spike S-K1: the toast types load in `powershell.exe` 5.1 but "a toast was not shown"; display and persistence are Inferred. SRE 4, SIM 3: a toast needs a signed-in, awake, un-muted desktop; the nightly window is when the operator sleeps. SRE 5: a failed `Invoke-RestMethod` in PS 5.1 carries the full URI in its message and `$_.TargetObject`, so an unhandled failure prints the topic against R6.8b(2). SRE 6, PAT 3: the wrapper is stateless, so a dead engine at 02:00 sends about 20 pushes before morning, and a failed delivery leaves no trace. SIM declines a dedupe state file for E3.

- **Item 1, ruling: one predicate; (a) and (b) refused.**
  - The defect is two definitions of "work left" (DM7), not a missing exception. `has_work(plan, rows)` is true iff any of: a cell with `launch_intent` and no `cell.outcome`; a cell with no `launch_intent` **and no stop row** (`stop_recorded(rows)`, D-K4; `run.launch_stopped` alone is not a stop, so a disk-low run has work and alarms, as §6.2 intends); a `cell.outcome` with no `cell.archived`; no `run.completed` after the latest `run.resumed` (or `run.started`). The resume's step 4 "nothing to do" is `not has_work`; the alarm's "pending > 0" is `has_work`. One function, one module (`resume.py`, run class; `alarm.py` imports it), no copy.
  - Under it a finished stop is silent by the record: intent cells have outcomes and archives, C7 cells are excluded by the stop row, and R-101's `run.completed` follows `run.resumed`. A crash mid-stop (intent cells without outcome) alarms until resumed. A crash in grading or before the last archive alarms (SRE 1 closed). A launch stop alarms. A named, never-started run alarms, as §6.2 arms it.
  - **(a) refused.** A `not_launched` (or `stopped`) outcome for a cell that never ran adds a value to ADR-0007's closed taxonomy for a fact the ledger already holds: the plan names the cell, `run.launch_stopped` names the stop, and no `launch_intent` exists. "n cells never launched" is derived (DM: derive, don't store), and `bench status` prints "stopped, n cells never launched" from exactly those rows. A stored outcome would also make a stopped cell and a never-launched cell indistinguishable in the facts, or add a second meaning to `stopped`.
  - **(b) refused as a separate alarm exception.** Excluding "runs whose stop fact holds" from the alarm would silence the mid-stop crash, which both the design and R-100 want loud, and it would be a second definition beside the resume's. The stop row belongs *inside* the one definition of work, as above — which is what PAT 2's fix says.
  - **R-100 condition 6 reads, from here:** "Alarm and status read `resume.has_work`; neither carries a definition of pending of its own. After a finish-the-stop resume `has_work` is false, so the alarm is silent by the record; a crash mid-stop leaves `has_work` true and the alarm fires until someone resumes."
  - **Tests (X-K1, red first):** `test_finished_stop_is_silent` holds a C7 cell in its fixture (red today, PAT 2); `test_alarm_fires_after_crash_in_grading` and `..._before_last_archive` (red today, SRE 1); `test_launch_stop_alarms`; a test that `alarm.check` has no pending logic of its own (the function is imported, M-ALARMPENDING mutant: a local copy fails). `bench status` prints "stopped, n cells never launched" (SRE 2's wording).

- **Item 2, ruling: ntfy primary, required when unattended; toast optional at the drill; egress accepted and bounded.**
  - The ADR's own test is "reaches the operator", and the operator it must reach is asleep. The spike shows a toast *built*, not *shown*; its display needs a signed-in, awake, un-muted session, which is the opposite of the nightly window. A channel that is off by default does not satisfy a precondition that is hard (ADR §7: "a hard precondition of E5"). So the push is primary, and the toast's "at minimum" wording in ADR-0021 §7 is amended to "a push the operator acknowledges at the drill, at minimum; a desktop toast is an optional local echo".
  - **Egress accepted, bounded.** One HTTPS POST per alert to `https://ntfy.sh/<topic>` (or a self-hosted base URL from `HB_ALARM_NTFY_URL`, optional) carrying only the run id, the `HB-ALM` code, the cause text and the age. No task content, path, cell id, credential or hostname. The topic is a bearer secret: a user environment variable only, never in the plan, ledger, repo, logs or output (R6.8b(2)). The Owner accepts this egress for the alarm only; nothing else in the bench may reuse the channel without a ruling.
  - **Unattended means required.** With `HB_ALARM_NTFY_TOPIC` unset the wrapper exits non-zero with the fixed line "alarm channel not configured: set HB_ALARM_NTFY_TOPIC (runbook)" and writes one event-log line; `bench campaign register` for a multi-night grid refuses until the drill record exists (ADR §7 already), and the drill cannot pass without the push. An attended single-night run may use the toast alone, and the runbook says in those words that a toast wakes no one.
  - **E3 ships the push; the toast joins at the drill (SIM 3).** X-K2's E3 wrapper is: run the check, POST on the edge, log. The toast branch (WinRT, AUMID, XML, 5.1 constraint) is added at the E5 drill if the operator wants the local echo; it is not on E3's path and its `powershell.exe` 5.1 constraint stays Inferred until then.
  - **The wrapper alerts on the edge (SRE 6, PAT 3; SIM's decline overruled on the SRE lens).** One small state file beside `.alarm_check` (outside the repo) holding `{code, first_sent_at, last_sent_at}`: send on a new code, re-send at most hourly while it persists, send once on recovery (exit 0 after a non-zero) and clear. Twenty pushes a night trains the operator to mute the only channel; the cost is a dozen lines of PowerShell. A delivery log, one line per wrapper run — time, exit code, push ok or "push failed: <exception type>" — with no topic and no URI, at a path the runbook names; `bench status` does not read it (it is the wrapper's record, SRE 11's sink).
  - **Never print the topic (SRE 5).** The POST is wrapped in `try/catch` that writes the fixed text and never `$_`, its message or `TargetObject`. Test: `test_alarm_task.py::test_push_failure_does_not_print_topic` with a stub `Invoke-RestMethod` that throws with the URL in its message; assert the topic is absent from stdout, stderr and the delivery log (red fixture: the script without the catch). The dry-run test takes the network branch through the stub.

- **Conditions:**
  1. **ADR-0021 Amendment 1 (the Coordinator's, R-100 condition 1) gains two lines**, decision text unchanged: §7 "while cells are pending" reads "while `resume.has_work` holds"; "a Windows toast at minimum, and a push ... if one is configured" reads "a push to the operator's phone the operator acknowledges at the drill, at minimum; a desktop toast is an optional local echo". Lands with W1-K's gate merge.
  2. **W1-K rev 1.2** (its gate revision): §3.1 step 4 and §6.2 cite `has_work`; §6.3 is rewritten to item 2 (channel table: ntfy primary and required unattended; toast optional at the drill; the edge state file; the delivery log; the catch); §13 moves the toast out of X-K2's E3 commits; the runbook outline gains the ntfy setup (install the app, choose a random topic, set the user variable, the self-host option), the delivery-log path and "a toast wakes no one". `bench status` wording "stopped, n cells never launched".
  3. **X-K1 / X-K2:** `has_work` is X-K1's (`resume.py`); `alarm.py` (X-K2) imports it; the tests above red-first; `tools/alarm-task.ps1` per item 2 with the dry-run test exercising the stub POST and the state file (two runs in a row send once; SRE 6's test). Task Scheduler flags from SRE 4 (start when available, no stop on battery, wake to run, run only when signed in) go in the runbook's `schtasks` line.
  4. **Operator, told, with one action before E5 (for the Leader's next report):** "The alarm's primary channel is a phone push through ntfy: before the E5 comparison grid, install the ntfy app, choose a random topic name, set the user environment variable `HB_ALARM_NTFY_TOPIC`, and run the drill (`bench campaign register` refuses a multi-night grid until the drill is recorded). The push carries only the run id, alarm code, cause and age. If you refuse third-party egress, say so: the fallback is a self-hosted ntfy, or toast-only for attended nights with the recorded residual that it wakes no one. R-102."

- **Reversibility:** item 1 high (no code; one function to name). Item 2 high until the drill is recorded; after it the channel is the campaign's recorded precondition and a change is a new drill.
